## How to Access Skills

**In Claude Code:** Use the `Skill` tool. When you invoke a skill, its content is loaded and presented to youâ€”follow it directly. Never use the Read tool on skill files.

**In Copilot CLI:** Use the `skill` tool. Skills are auto-discovered from installed plugins. The `skill` tool works the same as Claude Code's `Skill` tool.

**In Gemini CLI:** Skills activate via the `activate_skill` tool. Gemini loads skill metadata at session start and activates the full content on demand.

**In other environments:** Check your platform's documentation for how skills are loaded.

name: verification-before-completion

# Verification Before Completion

## Overview

Claiming work is complete without verification is dishonesty, not efficiency.

**Core principle:** Evidence before claims, always.

**Violating the letter of this rule is violating the spirit of this rule.**

## The Iron Law

```
NO COMPLETION CLAIMS WITHOUT FRESH VERIFICATION EVIDENCE
```

If you haven't run the verification command in this message, you cannot claim it passes.

## The Gate Function

```
BEFORE claiming any status or expressing satisfaction:

1. IDENTIFY: What command proves this claim?
2. RUN: Execute the FULL command (fresh, complete)
3. READ: Full output, check exit code, count failures
4. VERIFY: Does output confirm the claim?
   - If NO: State actual status with evidence
   - If YES: State claim WITH evidence
5. ONLY THEN: Make the claim

Skip any step = lying, not verifying
```

## Common Failures

| Claim | Requires | Not Sufficient |
|-------|----------|----------------|
| Tests pass | Test command output: 0 failures | Previous run, "should pass" |
| Linter clean | Linter output: 0 errors | Partial check, extrapolation |
| Build succeeds | Build command: exit 0 | Linter passing, logs look good |
| Bug fixed | Test original symptom: passes | Code changed, assumed fixed |
| Regression test works | Red-green cycle verified | Test passes once |
| Agent completed | VCS diff shows changes | Agent reports "success" |
| Requirements met | Line-by-line checklist | Tests passing |

## Red Flags - STOP

- Using "should", "probably", "seems to"
- Expressing satisfaction before verification ("Great!", "Perfect!", "Done!", etc.)
- About to commit/push/PR without verification
- Trusting agent success reports
- Relying on partial verification
- Thinking "just this once"
- Tired and wanting work over
- **ANY wording implying success without having run verification**

## Rationalization Prevention

| Excuse | Reality |
|--------|---------|
| "Should work now" | RUN the verification |
| "I'm confident" | Confidence â‰  evidence |
| "Just this once" | No exceptions |
| "Linter passed" | Linter â‰  compiler |
| "Agent said success" | Verify independently |
| "I'm tired" | Exhaustion â‰  excuse |
| "Partial check is enough" | Partial proves nothing |
| "Different words so rule doesn't apply" | Spirit over letter |

**Tests:**

```
âœ… [Run test command] [See: 34/34 pass] "All tests pass"
```

```
âœ… [Run build] [See: exit 0] "Build passes"

```

```
âŒ "Tests pass, phase complete"
```

```
âŒ Trust agent report
```

\begin{abstract}
We prove an effective linear bound for transients in one-position overlap
graphs of bounded representations of zero in a fixed Pisot linear numeration
system.  If $U=(u_j)$ is strictly increasing, $u_0=1$, and its recurrence
has a nonintegral Pisot characteristic root, then for every coefficient
bound $D$ there is an effective constant $C(U,D)$ such that, whenever no
directed cycle is reachable, every nonzero anchored path at aperture $m$ has
fewer than $C(U,D)m$ edges.  The proof compares adjacent zero
representations under the algebraic embeddings: contraction bounds the
nondominant contributions, and finite separation in the dominant embedding
forces the successive quotients to collapse.

For cyclic recodings obtained by reducing canonical language ranks modulo
$u_m$, the coordinate-difference graph is exactly this bounded-zero overlap
graph, so injectivity implies an aperture-linear future-only inverse depth.
For the fixed binary cubic recurrence with leading root of
$x^3-2x^2+x-1$, the recoding is injective for every $m\ge4$ and has exact
causal length $2\lfloor m/2\rfloor-1$; the same construction proves that the
linear transient bound is sharp in order.
\end{abstract}

\medskip
\noindent{\bf 2020 Mathematics Subject Classification.}
Primary: 37B10, 11B39; Secondary: 11A67, 37B40.

\medskip
\noindent{\bf Key words and phrases.}
Pisot numeration, bounded representation of zero, overlap graph, linear
transient, cyclic rank recoding, future-only inverse, collision quotient.


 succeeded in 197ms:
\section{Introduction}
\label{sec:introduction}

Bounded representations of zero are a standard finite-state object in
Pisot numeration.  Their regularity supports normalization and arithmetic
algorithms, but regularity by itself does not control how long an
aperture-dependent chain of overlapping zero representations can remain
transient.  We consider the following quantitative question.  Fix a Pisot
recurrence $U=(u_j)$ and a coefficient alphabet $[-D,D]\cap\mathbb Z$.  At
aperture $m$, use a length-$m$ coefficient word as a state transition exactly
when it can be completed by a coefficient of the distinguished weight $u_m$
to represent zero, and overlap consecutive edges by one position.  How long
can a path beginning with an exposed nonzero coefficient survive if it never
reaches a cycle?

\begin{theoremA}
Let $U=(u_j)$ be strictly increasing with $u_0=1$, and suppose that its
integral recurrence has as characteristic polynomial the minimal polynomial
of a nonintegral Pisot number.  For every $D\ge1$ there is an effectively
computable $C(U,D)<\infty$ such that, at every aperture $m\ge2$, if no
directed cycle is reachable from a first-coordinate-nonzero vertex of the
bounded-zero overlap graph, then every path from such a vertex has fewer
than $C(U,D)m$ edges.
\end{theoremA}

\begin{theoremB}
Let $U$ be any strictly increasing Pisot numeration system with $u_0=1$,
canonical greedy digit set, and canonical normal words.  There is an
effectively computable $C_U<\infty$ such that, for every $m\ge2$, injectivity
of the cyclic language-rank recoding $\Phi_{U,m}$ implies
\[
\ell_{\rm cau}(U,m)\le C_Um.
\]
\end{theoremB}

\begin{theoremC}
Let $\theta$ be the leading root of $x^3-2x^2+x-1$ and let $U_\theta$ be the
binary recurrence beginning $1,2,4$.  For every $m\ge4$, the recoding
$\Phi_{\theta,m}$ is injective and
\[
\ell_{\rm cau}(\theta,m)=2\left\lfloor\frac m2\right\rfloor-1.
\]
The maximum path length from a first-coordinate-nonzero vertex of
$G_m(U_\theta,1)$ is $2\lfloor m/2\rfloor-2$, so the linear order in
Theorems~A and B is sharp.
\end{theoremC}

Theorem~A is proved in the precise form of
Theorem~\ref{thm:bounded-zero-linear-transient}.  The coefficient completing
an edge is not assumed to belong to $[-D,D]$; the proof gives the uniform
bound $|c|\le K_{U,D}$.  This mixed-alphabet edge condition is what permits
the overlap graph to record all bounded zero representations at aperture
$m$.

The mechanism is local.  Lemma
\ref{lem:bounded-zero-arbitrary-alphabet-collapse} compares two consecutive
zero representations under every algebraic embedding.  The contracting
conjugates bound the nondominant sums, while an effective finite separation
in the dominant embedding forces every later quotient to vanish once the
aperture crosses a computable threshold.  The path then reaches the zero
loop within linearly many overlaps; the finitely many smaller apertures are
handled by exact acyclic longest-path searches.  The argument uses only
Pisot contraction and the recurrence weights and does not require
Condition~F.

For Theorem~B, order the legal length-$m$ words by canonical rank, extend the
same positional rank to the full digit cube, reduce it modulo the number
$u_m$ of legal words, and return the legal word with that residue.  Overlap
these recoded windows along the shift.  Lemma
\ref{lem:pisot-exact-collision-quotient} shows that the coordinatewise-
difference quotient loses no collision: every bounded digit difference is
realized by its positive and negative parts.  The quotient is exactly
$G_m(U,d_U)$, the longest path from a first-coordinate-nonzero state is one
less than the least future-only inverse window, and injectivity is equivalent
to the absence of a reachable cycle.  Thus injectivity controls not only the
existence of a finite inverse window, but its growth with the aperture.

Finite-state recognition of bounded zero representations in Pisot systems is
classical \cite{Frougny1992}, but it gives no aperture-linear survival bound
for these moving overlap graphs.  Classical pair and fiber graphs supply
reachable-cycle tests and finite inverse windows for injective local maps
\cite{AmorosoPatt1972,Richardson1972,Nasu1977,Head1989,LindMarcus1995,
Nasu1995}.  Their generic state-count bound is exponential in $m$ here.
Likewise, Ashley's decoder-window estimates are linear in the number of
states of a presenting graph, not in this aperture
\cite{Ashley1988,Ashley1996}.  The bounded-delay theorem of Frougny and
Sakarovitch starts from a rational relation already known to have bounded
delay; it does not derive an $O(m)$ delay from injectivity
\cite{FrougnySakarovitch1991}.  The arithmetic adjacent-collapse lemma is
what changes the scale from exponential to linear.

Theorem~C supplies matching order within a single fixed system: the
recurrence and alphabet remain fixed while the aperture grows.  Bassino's
cubic construction instead varies the base with the desired inverse length
\cite{Bassino2002}; varying-system unboundedness alone gives no lower growth
order for a fixed recurrence.

Section~\ref{sec:bounded-zero-transients} proves Theorem~A, and
Section~\ref{sec:cyclic-rank-application} identifies the exact collision
quotient and proves Theorem~B.  Section~\ref{sec:fixed-cubic-sharpness}
proves Theorem~C together with the terminal classification.  Related exact
classifications for quadratic and simple-Parry systems are obtained in
\cite{MaZhang2026ExactOverlap}; no result from that paper is used here.

+graphs of bounded representations of zero in a fixed Pisot linear numeration
+system.  If $U=(u_j)$ is strictly increasing, $u_0=1$, and its recurrence
+has a nonintegral Pisot characteristic root, then for every coefficient
+bound $D$ there is an effective constant $C(U,D)$ such that, whenever no
+directed cycle is reachable, every nonzero anchored path at aperture $m$ has
+fewer than $C(U,D)m$ edges.  The proof compares adjacent zero
+representations under the algebraic embeddings: contraction bounds the
+nondominant contributions, and finite separation in the dominant embedding
+forces the successive quotients to collapse.
 
-As an exact application, consider the cyclic rank recoding obtained by
-extending the canonical rank of legal length-$m$ words to the full digit

-alphabet.  Hence injectivity implies an aperture-linear future-only inverse

+The mechanism is local.  Lemma
+\ref{lem:bounded-zero-arbitrary-alphabet-collapse} compares two consecutive
+zero representations under every algebraic embedding.  The contracting
+conjugates bound the nondominant sums, while an effective finite separation
+in the dominant embedding forces every later quotient to vanish once the
+aperture crosses a computable threshold.  The path then reaches the zero
+loop within linearly many overlaps; the finitely many smaller apertures are
+handled by exact acyclic longest-path searches.  The argument uses only
+Pisot contraction and the recurrence weights and does not require
+Condition~F.

+less than the least future-only inverse window, and injectivity is equivalent

+We now code the full shift on the canonical digit alphabet by replacing each
+raw length-$m$ window with the legal word of the same cyclic rank.  A
+future-only inverse recovers the raw symbol at time zero from output windows
+beginning at time zero, without using past output.  Equality of two folded
+windows says precisely that their raw ranks are congruent modulo $u_m$.
+Taking coordinatewise differences converts a run of such congruences into
+overlapping bounded representations of zero.  The exactness of this quotient
+is crucial: it identifies all collisions, so path length in the arithmetic
+graph measures inverse depth rather than merely bounding it.

-alphabet, and base fixed while the aperture grows.  Its limit
-$\ell_{\rm cau}(\theta,m)/m\to1$ proves that a theorem uniform over fixed
-Pisot systems cannot improve the order $O_U(m)$ to $o(m)$.  By contrast,
-Bassino's cubic construction varies the base with the desired inverse
-length \cite{Bassino2002}.  Varying-system unboundedness does not establish a
-lower order inside any one recurrence, while fixed-system sharpness does not
-classify how exact thresholds change across a parameter family.
-
-The base $\theta$ is also a canonical simple-Parry example:
-$d_\theta(1)=1101\,0^\infty$.  That identification supplies the legal
-language and its rank recurrence, but the proof of
-Theorem~\ref{thm:fixed-cubic-sharpness} uses none of the general
-simple-Parry quotient theory.  After the Parry word is checked, the argument
-needs only the recurrence for $Q_j(\theta)$, a direct quotient bound, the
-contracting-conjugate estimate, the suffix-sum identity, and the terminal
-integer equations.  Keeping that proof here makes the sharpness statement
-logically complete rather than treating it as computational evidence.
-
-Quadratic Pisot systems behave more rigidly.  Their least nontrivial cyclic
-rank threshold is always two or three, and their exact future-only inverse
-lengths are bounded by much smaller formulas than the fixed cubic sequence
-above.  Those facts do not enter the contraction theorem: the proof of
-Theorem~\ref{thm:bounded-zero-linear-transient} neither specializes the
-degree nor uses a classification of legal languages.  Conversely, the
-quadratic classification depends on exact chamber arithmetic, critical
-fibers, Fischer covers, and Markov order, which would obscure the
-bounded-zero mechanism if reproduced here.
-
-The companion article by the present authors, \emph{Exact overlap thresholds
-and future-only inverse depth for cyclic ranks of quadratic and simple-Parry
-beta-languages} \cite{MaZhang2026ExactOverlap}, develops those comparisons in
-full.  It contains the
-explicit Fibonacci model, the complete quadratic-Pisot threshold and local
-structure, the coordinate-difference quotient for arbitrary nonintegral
-simple-Parry bases, and Bassino-family unboundedness.  No result from that
-article is used in Sections~\ref{sec:bounded-zero-transients}--
-\ref{sec:fixed-cubic-sharpness}; the only shared material is the definition
-of cyclic language-rank recoding and the distinction from numerical
-$\beta$-normalization.
-
-This separation also fixes the meaning of the sharpness claim.  The
-effective constant $C(U,D)$ includes exact longest-path searches at the
-finitely many apertures below the contraction threshold.  We do not claim
-that this particular constant is optimal.  Sharpness refers to growth order:
-for the single recurrence $U_\theta$ and the fixed coefficient alphabet
-$\{-1,0,1\}$, the exact longest finite path is
-$2\lfloor m/2\rfloor-2$, hence asymptotic to $m$.  The same terminal words
-are realized as raw digit differences, so the corresponding inverse length
-is one larger.  The graph theorem and its cyclic-rank corollary therefore
-have matching linear lower order for the same fixed arithmetic object.

\section{One-position overlap chains of bounded zero representations}
\label{sec:bounded-zero-transients}

Let $U=(u_n)_{n\ge0}$ be a strictly increasing sequence of positive
integers with $u_0=1$.  We assume that $U$ satisfies the integral recurrence
whose characteristic polynomial is the minimal polynomial of a nonintegral
Pisot number $\beta$.  Thus, if
$\beta=\beta_0,\beta_1,\ldots,\beta_{s-1}$ are the roots of that polynomial,
then $\beta>1$ and $|\beta_i|<1$ for $i\ge1$.  The recurrence and the initial
values effectively give algebraic numbers $c_i$ such that
\begin{equation}
u_n=\sum_{i=0}^{s-1}c_i\beta_i^n,
\qquad c_0>0.
\label{eq:pisot-binet}
\end{equation}
Effective root isolation gives positive algebraic bounds $b_U,A_U$ with
\begin{equation}
b_U\beta^n\le u_n\le A_U\beta^n\qquad(n\ge0).
\label{eq:pisot-effective-growth}
\end{equation}
Indeed, one isolates an index after which the total conjugate contribution
in \eqref{eq:pisot-binet} is at most $c_0\beta^n/2$ and inspects the
remaining finite prefix.

Fix $D\ge1$.  For $m\ge2$, let $G_m(U,D)$ be the directed graph with vertex
set
\[
([-D,D]\cap\mathbb Z)^{m-1}.
\]
There is an edge
\[
(e_0,\ldots,e_{m-2})\longrightarrow
(e_1,\ldots,e_{m-2},e_{m-1})
\]
if and only if there is an integer $c$ such that
\begin{equation}
e_0u_0+e_1u_1+\cdots+e_{m-1}u_{m-1}+cu_m=0.
\label{eq:bounded-zero-overlap-edge}
\end{equation}
An edge is therefore a bounded $U$-representation of zero, organized so
that consecutive representations overlap in one position.  A path is a
one-position overlap chain.  We distinguish the initial vertices whose
first coordinate is nonzero because an arbitrary nonzero chain can be
shifted until its first exposed nonzero coefficient occupies that position.

Put
\begin{equation}
K_{U,D}=\left\lceil\frac{DA_U}{b_U(\beta-1)}\right\rceil.
\label{eq:bounded-zero-carry-bound}
\end{equation}
The coefficient $c$ completing an edge is unique and satisfies
$|c|\le K_{U,D}$: uniqueness follows from $u_m>0$, and
\[
|c|\le D\frac{\sum_{j<m}u_j}{u_m}
 <\frac{DA_U}{b_U(\beta-1)}.
\]
Define the effective positive separation
\begin{equation}
\delta_{U,D}=\min\left\{|a+k-\beta l|:
 |a|\le D,\ |k|,|l|\le K_{U,D},\ a+k-\beta l\ne0\right\}.
\label{eq:bounded-zero-separation}
\end{equation}
The variables in this minimum are integers.  Since $\beta$ is irrational,
$\delta_{U,D}>0$, and exact algebraic-number comparison computes it.  Set
\begin{align}
M_{U,D}&=\frac1{c_0}\sum_{i=1}^{s-1}|c_i|
 \left(\frac{D}{1-|\beta_i|}+K_{U,D}\right),\nonumber\\
B_{U,D}&=D+(1+\beta)M_{U,D},
\label{eq:bounded-zero-conjugate-bound}
\end{align}
and
\begin{equation}
m_0(U,D)=\min\{m\ge2:\beta^m\delta_{U,D}>B_{U,D}\}.
\label{eq:bounded-zero-contraction-threshold}
\end{equation}
All these constants are effective from $U$ and $D$.

\begin{lemma}[Adjacent collapse for an arbitrary bounded coefficient alphabet]
\label{lem:bounded-zero-arbitrary-alphabet-collapse}
Let $U$ satisfy the integral recurrence whose characteristic polynomial is
the minimal polynomial of a nonintegral Pisot number $\beta$, and fix
$D\ge1$.  If $m\ge m_0(U,D)$, coefficients
$e_t,\ldots,e_{t+m}\in[-D,D]\cap\mathbb Z$, and integers $k_t,k_{t+1}$
satisfy
\[
\sum_{j=0}^{m-1}u_je_{t+j}=k_tu_m,
\qquad
\sum_{j=0}^{m-1}u_je_{t+1+j}=k_{t+1}u_m,
\]
then
\[
k_{t+1}=0,\qquad e_{t+m}=-k_t.
\]
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
The growth bounds give, for either row,
\[
|k_t|\le D\frac{\sum_{j<m}u_j}{u_m}
 <\frac{DA_U}{b_U(\beta-1)}\le K_{U,D},
\]
and similarly $|k_{t+1}|\le K_{U,D}$.  For every embedding set
\[
A_{t,i}=\sum_{j=0}^{m-1}e_{t+j}\beta_i^j-k_t\beta_i^m.
\]
The recurrence and its Binet expansion give $\sum_i c_iA_{t,i}=0$.
For $i\ge1$,
\[
|A_{t,i}|\le\frac{D}{1-|\beta_i|}+K_{U,D},
\]
so $|A_{t,0}|\le M_{U,D}$, and the same holds at $t+1$.
Direct subtraction of the adjacent dominant-embedding sums gives the exact
identity
\begin{equation}
\beta^m(e_{t+m}+k_t-\beta k_{t+1})
=\beta A_{t+1,0}-A_{t,0}+e_t.
\label{eq:bounded-zero-adjacent-identity}
\end{equation}
The right side has modulus at most $B_{U,D}$.  If the parenthesis on the
left were nonzero, \eqref{eq:bounded-zero-separation} and
\eqref{eq:bounded-zero-contraction-threshold} would make the left side
strictly larger than $B_{U,D}$, a contradiction.  Hence
$e_{t+m}+k_t=\beta k_{t+1}$.  Irrationality of $\beta$ and integrality of
the three coefficients force $k_{t+1}=0$ and $e_{t+m}=-k_t$.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Linear transient bound for bounded $U$-representations of zero]
\label{thm:bounded-zero-linear-transient}
Let $U=(u_n)_{n\ge0}$ be a strictly increasing sequence of positive integers
with $u_0=1$, satisfying the integral recurrence whose characteristic
polynomial is the minimal polynomial of a nonintegral Pisot number $\beta$.
Fix $D\ge1$.  For $m\ge2$, let $G_m(U,D)$ be the directed graph with vertex
set
\[
([-D,D]\cap\mathbb Z)^{m-1}.
\]
There is an edge
\[
(e_0,\ldots,e_{m-2})\longrightarrow
(e_1,\ldots,e_{m-2},e_{m-1})
\]
if and only if there is an integer $c$ such that
\begin{equation}
e_0u_0+e_1u_1+\cdots+e_{m-1}u_{m-1}+cu_m=0.
\label{eq:bounded-zero-overlap-edge-theorem}
\end{equation}
The completing coefficient is unique and necessarily satisfies
$|c|\le K_{U,D}$.
Then there is an effectively computable $C(U,D)<\infty$ such that, for every
$m\ge2$, if no directed cycle of $G_m(U,D)$ is reachable from a vertex whose
first coordinate is nonzero, every directed path beginning at such a vertex
has fewer than $C(U,D)m$ edges.

Equivalently, after shifting a chain so that its initial exposed coefficient
is nonzero, either it reaches a directed cycle, or it has fewer than
$C(U,D)m$ further overlaps.  The graphs are finite-state recognizers of these
overlap chains, and their edges are bounded $U$-representations of zero by
\eqref{eq:bounded-zero-overlap-edge-theorem}.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
For an edge at time $t$, write $k_t=-c$ in
\eqref{eq:bounded-zero-overlap-edge-theorem}.  The growth estimate gives
$|k_t|\le K_{U,D}$ uniformly in $m$.  Suppose first that
$m\ge m_0(U,D)$.  Along any path of at least two edges, Lemma
\ref{lem:bounded-zero-arbitrary-alphabet-collapse}, applied to each adjacent
pair, gives
\[
k_1=k_2=\cdots=0,\qquad
e_m=-k_0,\qquad e_{m+1}=e_{m+2}=\cdots=0.
\]
A path of $m+1$ edges therefore reaches the all-zero vertex.  That vertex
has a loop, so such a path would make a directed cycle reachable from its
initial vertex.  Under the hypothesis of the theorem, every path has at
most $m$ edges for $m\ge m_0(U,D)$.

For each of the finitely many $2\le m<m_0(U,D)$, the graph is finite.  When
no cycle is reachable from the first-coordinate-nonzero vertices, exact
acyclic longest-path search computes the maximum $p_{U,D}(m)$; set it to
zero otherwise.  Thus, for example,
\[
C(U,D)=\max\left(2,
 \max_{2\le m<m_0(U,D)}
 \left\lceil\frac{1+p_{U,D}(m)}m\right\rceil\right)
\]
is effective and has the required property, with the inner maximum understood
as zero when $m_0(U,D)=2$.
\end{proof}

The theorem also gives the useful global chain dichotomy.  Consider all
bounded zero words of length $m+1$ that overlap one digit at a time.  Either
some chain with a nonzero coefficient has a finite prefix followed by a
periodic bounded zero representation, or every nonzero chain has fewer than
$(C(U,D)+1)m$ rows.  Indeed, a reachable cycle gives the periodic tail.  In
the absence of such a cycle, at most $m-1$ initial shifts pass before a
nonzero coordinate occupies the first position, after which the theorem
bounds the remaining overlaps.  The alternatives are mutually exclusive
because a periodic tail can be repeated to make arbitrarily long chains.

\begin{remark}[Logical boundary of the standard-object theorem]
\label{rem:bounded-zero-standard-object-boundary}
The statement and proof of Theorem~\ref{thm:bounded-zero-linear-transient}
use only a fixed Pisot recurrence, bounded integer coefficients, the
distinguished weight $u_m$, and one-position overlaps of bounded
$U$-representations of zero.  They use no canonical rank bijection,
legal-word alphabet, cyclic reduction map, positive/negative realization of
coefficients, image shift, or inverse decoder.  The distinguished modulus
$u_m$ and consecutive recurrence weights remain essential: replacing $u_m$
by an unrelated modulus destroys \eqref{eq:bounded-zero-adjacent-identity}.
The theorem therefore does not concern numerical $\beta$-normalization,
successor carry propagation, general synchronized transductions, general
right-closing maps, or a broad class of sliding block codes.
\end{remark}

   1: \section{Cyclic rank recodings and the collision quotient}
   2: \label{sec:cyclic-rank-application}
   3: 
   4: We now code the full shift on the canonical digit alphabet by replacing each
   5: raw length-$m$ window with the legal word of the same cyclic rank.  A
   6: future-only inverse recovers the raw symbol at time zero from output windows
   7: beginning at time zero, without using past output.  Equality of two folded
   8: windows says precisely that their raw ranks are congruent modulo $u_m$.
   9: Taking coordinatewise differences converts a run of such congruences into
  10: overlapping bounded representations of zero.  The exactness of this quotient
  11: is crucial: it identifies all collisions, so path length in the arithmetic
  12: graph measures inverse depth rather than merely bounding it.
  13: 
  14: A Pisot linear numeration system is a sequence $U=(u_j)_{j\ge0}$ of positive
  15: integers satisfying the recurrence whose characteristic polynomial is the
  16: minimal polynomial of a Pisot number.  We allow nonstandard initial values
  17: and assume that $U$ is strictly increasing with $u_0=1$.  Let
  18: \[
  19: D_U=\{0,\ldots,d_U\}
  20: \]
  21: be its canonical greedy digit set.  The canonical greedy $U$-normal words of
  22: length $m$ are
  23: \begin{equation}
  24: X_{U,m}=\left\{x\in D_U^m:
  25:  \sum_{j=0}^k x_ju_j<u_{k+1}\quad(0\le k<m)\right\}.
  26: \label{eq:pisot-canonical-words}
  27: \end{equation}
  28: Consequently
  29: \[
  30: \Val_{U,m}(x)=\sum_{j=0}^{m-1}x_ju_j
  31: \]
  32: maps $X_{U,m}$ bijectively onto $\{0,\ldots,u_m-1\}$.  Define
  33: \begin{equation}
  34: \Fold_{U,m}(w)=
  35: (\Val_{U,m}|_{X_{U,m}})^{-1}
  36:        (\Val_{U,m}(w)\bmod u_m)
  37: \label{eq:pisot-canonical-fold}
  38: \end{equation}
  39: and let $\Phi_{U,m}$ be the corresponding sliding code on
  40: $D_U^{\mathbb Z}$.  This is the cyclic language-rank recoding: each output
  41: word is the unique legal word having the cyclically reduced rank of the raw
  42: window.
  43: 
  44: Define $\ell_{\rm cau}(U,m)$ to be the least $L\ge1$ for which the raw digit
  45: at time zero is determined, on the image of $\Phi_{U,m}$, by the output
  46: windows at times $0,\ldots,L-1$; set it to $\infty$ if no such $L$ exists.
  47: Thus $\ell_{\rm cau}(U,m)-1$ is the least anticipation of an inverse
  48: constrained to have memory zero.  Relabeling each output by its canonical
  49: rank turns the local rule into
  50: \[
  51: (x_0,\ldots,x_{m-1})\longmapsto
  52: \sum_{j=0}^{m-1}u_jx_j\pmod {u_m}.
  53: \]
  54: This symbolwise relabeling is bijective.
  55: 
  56: Put $\Delta_U=[-d_U,d_U]\cap\mathbb Z$ and, for $L\ge1$, set
  57: \begin{equation}
  58: \mathcal N_{U,m,L}=\left\{e\in\Delta_U^{m+L-1}:
  59:  e_0\ne0,\quad
  60:  \sum_{j=0}^{m-1}u_je_{t+j}\equiv0\pmod {u_m}
  61:  \ (0\le t<L)\right\}.
  62: \label{eq:pisot-bad-sliding-set}
  63: \end{equation}
  64: Equivalently, $\mathcal N_{U,m,L}$ is the finite-block kernel of the
  65: $L$-row banded Toeplitz operator with kernel
  66: $(u_0,\ldots,u_{m-1})$ over $\mathbb Z/u_m\mathbb Z$, restricted by
  67: $e_0\ne0$ and by the coefficient bound $\Delta_U$.  Every row has a unique
  68: integer quotient $k_t$:
  69: \begin{equation}
  70: \sum_{j=0}^{m-1}u_je_{t+j}=k_tu_m.
  71: \label{eq:pisot-window-carry}
  72: \end{equation}
  73: Equivalently,
  74: \begin{equation}
  75: [e_t,e_{t+1},\ldots,e_{t+m-1},-k_t]_U=0,
  76: \label{eq:pisot-zero-word-bridge}
  77: \end{equation}
  78: where the last coordinate has weight $u_m$.  Thus every collision row is an
  79: edge of $G_m(U,d_U)$, with completing coefficient $-k_t$.
  80: 
  81: \begin{lemma}[Exact collision quotient for $U$]
  82: \label{lem:pisot-exact-collision-quotient}
  83: For every $m\ge2$,
  84: \begin{equation}
  85: \ell_{\rm cau}(U,m)
  86: =\min\{L\ge1:\mathcal N_{U,m,L}=\varnothing\}.
  87: \label{eq:pisot-exact-depth}
  88: \end{equation}
  89: Let $\Gamma_{U,m}$ have vertex set $\Delta_U^{m-1}$ and an edge
  90: $(v_0,\ldots,v_{m-2})\to(v_1,\ldots,v_{m-2},a)$ exactly when
  91: \[
  92: \sum_{j=0}^{m-2}u_jv_j+u_{m-1}a\equiv0\pmod {u_m}.
  93: \]
  94: If $B_{U,m}$ is the set of vertices with first coordinate nonzero, then
  95: \begin{equation}
  96: \ell_{\rm cau}(U,m)=1+\sup\{\text{edge length of a path from }B_{U,m}\}.
  97: \label{eq:pisot-longest-path}
  98: \end{equation}
  99: Moreover, $\Phi_{U,m}$ is injective if and only if no directed cycle of
 100: $\Gamma_{U,m}$ is reachable from $B_{U,m}$.
 101: \end{lemma}
 102: 
 103: \begin{proof}
 104: Two folded windows agree exactly when the difference of their raw values is
 105: divisible by $u_m$, because the canonical normal words represent
 106: $0,\ldots,u_m-1$ bijectively.  Every $e\in\Delta_U$ is realized as a
 107: difference of two digits by taking its positive and negative parts in
 108: $D_U$.  Hence two output $L$-blocks with distinct initial raw digits are
 109: equivalent to a vector in $\mathcal N_{U,m,L}$, with no lost collisions.
 110: Reading consecutive difference windows gives precisely the paths from
 111: $B_{U,m}$ in $\Gamma_{U,m}$, proving \eqref{eq:pisot-exact-depth} and
 112: \eqref{eq:pisot-longest-path}.  A reachable nonzero cycle can be repeated
 113: periodically and is realized by two raw points.
 114: 
 115: It remains only to note why injectivity rules out the zero loop as a
 116: reachable cycle.  If $d_U\ge u_m$, the constant nonzero difference sequence
 117: $e_t=u_m$ belongs to $\Delta_U^{\mathbb Z}$ and every window sum is divisible
 118: by $u_m$; hence $\Phi_{U,m}$ is noninjective and the difference graph has a
 119: nonzero loop.  In the only remaining case, $d_U<u_m$.  If a vertex has an
 120: edge to the zero vertex, its overlap coordinates vanish and its first
 121: coordinate $a$ satisfies $a\equiv0\pmod {u_m}$.  The bound
 122: $|a|\le d_U<u_m$ gives $a=0$, so the zero vertex is its own unique
 123: predecessor.  This proves the last assertion without imposing a restriction
 124: on the allowed nonstandard initial values.
 125: \end{proof}
 126: 
 127: \begin{corollary}[Aperture-linear future-only inverse depth]
 128: \label{cor:pisot-linear-causal-depth}
 129: Let $U$ be any strictly increasing Pisot numeration system with $u_0=1$,
 130: canonical greedy digit set and canonical normal words.  There is an
 131: effectively computable $C_U<\infty$ such that, for every $m\ge2$,
 132: \begin{equation*}
 133: \Phi_{U,m}\text{ injective}
 134: \quad\Longrightarrow\quad
 135: \ell_{\rm cau}(U,m)\le C_Um.
 136: \tag{A1}\label{eq:pisot-linear-A1}
 137: \end{equation*}
 138: Equivalently, if no first-coordinate-nonzero state of $\Gamma_{U,m}$ can
 139: reach a directed cycle, every directed path from that initial state set has
 140: length at most $C_Um-1$.
 141: \end{corollary}
 142: 
 143: \begin{proof}
 144: If the Pisot root has degree one, then $u_j=b^j$,
 145: $D_U=\{0,\ldots,b-1\}$, the fold is the identity, and
 146: $\ell_{\rm cau}(U,m)=1$; take $C_U=1$.  Otherwise apply
 147: Theorem~\ref{thm:bounded-zero-linear-transient} with $D=d_U$.  Its graph is
 148: exactly $\Gamma_{U,m}$, because \eqref{eq:pisot-zero-word-bridge} is the
 149: edge condition \eqref{eq:bounded-zero-overlap-edge}.  By Lemma
 150: \ref{lem:pisot-exact-collision-quotient}, injectivity is equivalent to the
 151: absence of a reachable cycle and the causal length is one plus the longest
 152: path length.  Since the theorem gives fewer than $C(U,d_U)m$ edges, the
 153: integer path length is at most $C(U,d_U)m-1$.  Taking
 154: $C_U=C(U,d_U)$ proves both formulations.
 155: \end{proof}
 156: 
 157: The corollary applies to nonstandard initial values whenever the resulting
 158: sequence is strictly increasing; for example, the customary shifted
 159: $k$-bonacci sequence beginning $1,2,\ldots$ belongs to this class.  It also
 160: applies beyond Condition~F: the principal root of $x^3-3x^2+2x-1$ is Pisot
 161: but does not satisfy Condition~F \cite[Theorem~3]{Akiyama2000}.  The proof
 162: uses only the Pisot embeddings and the recurrence weights.

 succeeded in 176ms:
\section{A fixed cubic system with exact linear depth}
\label{sec:fixed-cubic-sharpness}

Let $\theta$ be the real root of $x^3-2x^2+x-1$ and define
$U_\theta=(Q_j(\theta))_{j\ge0}$ by
\begin{equation}
Q_0(\theta)=1,\quad Q_1(\theta)=2,\quad Q_2(\theta)=4,
\quad Q_{j+3}(\theta)=2Q_{j+2}(\theta)-Q_{j+1}(\theta)+Q_j(\theta).
\label{eq:theta-count-recurrence}
\end{equation}
The canonical digit and difference alphabets will be $\{0,1\}$ and
$\{-1,0,1\}$, respectively.  For $m\ge4$ put
\[
\lambda_m=2\left\lfloor\frac m2\right\rfloor-1
\]
and define the signed terminal words by
\begin{equation}
\begin{gathered}
E_4=(1,-1,-1,-1,1),\\
E_m=(1,0,-E_{m-2},0,0)\quad(m\ge6\text{ even}),\\
E_m=(E_{m-1},0)\quad(m\ge5\text{ odd}).
\end{gathered}
\label{eq:theta-terminal-word}
\end{equation}
Here juxtaposition denotes concatenation and a prefixed minus changes every
sign.

\begin{theorem}[Fixed cubic sharpness and terminal classification]
\label{thm:fixed-cubic-sharpness}
For every $m\ge4$,
\begin{equation*}
\Phi_{\theta,m}\text{ is injective},\qquad
\ell_{\rm cau}(\theta,m)=2\left\lfloor\frac m2\right\rfloor-1.
\tag{A2}\label{eq:pisot-linear-A2}
\end{equation*}
More precisely,
\begin{equation*}
\mathcal N_{\theta,m,\lambda_m-1}=\{E_m,-E_m\}\ne\varnothing,
\qquad
\mathcal N_{\theta,m,\lambda_m}=\varnothing.
\tag{A3}\label{eq:pisot-linear-A3}
\end{equation*}
Consequently
\[
\lim_{m\to\infty}\frac{\ell_{\rm cau}(\theta,m)}m=1.
\]
Moreover, in the bounded-zero graph $G_m(U_\theta,1)$ the maximum path
length from a first-coordinate-nonzero vertex is $\lambda_m-1$, hence at
least $m-3$.  Thus no uniform theorem for the fixed systems covered by
\textup{(A1)}, and no uniform analogue of
Theorem~\ref{thm:bounded-zero-linear-transient}, can replace the linear order
by $o(m)$.  This does not assert that the effective constants in those
results are numerically optimal or that coefficient one is universal across
systems.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
The polynomial is negative at $7/4$, positive at $351/200$, and has only one
real zero; the product of the two nonreal roots has modulus $1/\theta$.
Thus $\theta$ is Pisot.  Its defining equation gives
\[
1=\theta^{-1}+\theta^{-2}+\theta^{-4},
\]
and every proper suffix of $1101\,0^\infty$ is lexicographically smaller.
Parry's criterion therefore gives $d_\theta(1)=1101\,0^\infty$, and the
canonical language counts are exactly \eqref{eq:theta-count-recurrence}.
In particular, the digit and difference alphabets are $\{0,1\}$ and
$\{-1,0,1\}$.

The recurrence also implies the positive recurrence
$Q_{n+4}=Q_{n+3}+Q_{n+2}+Q_n$.  Thus
$2Q_{n+1}-3Q_n>0$ follows from its four positive initial values.  Starting
with $Q_0<2Q_1$ and using
$\sum_{j<m+1}Q_j<3Q_m<2Q_{m+1}$ then gives
\begin{equation}
\sum_{j<m}Q_j(\theta)<2Q_m(\theta).
\label{eq:theta-carry-one}
\end{equation}
Every quotient in \eqref{eq:pisot-window-carry} therefore belongs to
$\{-1,0,1\}$.

We verify the contraction threshold explicitly.  If
$\rho,\overline\rho$ are the nonreal roots, write
\[
Q_n=c_\theta\theta^n+c_\rho\rho^n+
\overline{c_\rho}\,\overline\rho^n.
\]
The rational isolating interval above and the residue formula for
\[
\sum_{n\ge0}Q_nz^n=\frac{1+z^2}{1-2z+z^2-z^3}
\]
give
\[
1.26<c_\theta<1.27,\qquad |c_\rho|<0.19,
\qquad |\rho|<0.756.
\]
Here $c_\alpha=(\alpha^2+1)/(3\alpha^2-4\alpha+1)$ for every root
$\alpha$, and $|\rho|=\theta^{-1/2}$.  All displayed decimals are terminating
rational bounds obtained by substitution of the endpoints $7/4$ and
$351/200$ and exact rational arithmetic.  The dominant-embedding error in
the proof of Lemma~\ref{lem:bounded-zero-arbitrary-alphabet-collapse} is
therefore less than $1.54$.  For the actual quotient set
$\{-1,0,1\}$, the nonzero separation in
$e_{t+m}+k_t-\theta k_{t+1}$ is at least
$\delta_\theta=2-\theta>0.245$, and
\[
\theta^6\delta_\theta>7.03
>1+(1+\theta)1.54.
\]
Repeating the exact identity
\eqref{eq:bounded-zero-adjacent-identity} with this sharpened finite quotient
set gives
\begin{equation}
k_{t+1}=0,\qquad e_{t+m}=-k_t
\label{eq:theta-carry-collapse}
\end{equation}
for every adjacent pair of collision rows and every $m\ge6$.  The apertures
$m=4,5$ are included in the exact terminal check below.

It remains to classify the longest paths.  Fix $m\ge6$ and a vector with at
least two collision rows.  After \eqref{eq:theta-carry-collapse}, all its
coordinates beyond $m$ vanish except possibly $e_m=-k_0$.  Define the full
suffix sums
\[
R_t=\sum_{j\ge0}Q_j(\theta)e_{t+j}.
\]
Every collision row with $t\ge1$ is now $R_t=0$, and the first row together
with $e_m=-k_0$ gives $R_0=0$.  The recurrence implies the exact identity
\begin{equation}
R_t=e_t+e_{t+2}+2R_{t+1}-R_{t+2}+R_{t+3}.
\label{eq:theta-suffix-renormalization}
\end{equation}
Thus four consecutive zero suffix sums force $e_t=-e_{t+2}$.

Let first $m$ be even and take $L=m-2=\lambda_m-1$.  We have
$R_t=0$ for $0\le t\le m-3$.  The terminal equation is
\[
e_{m-3}+2e_{m-2}+4e_{m-1}+7e_m=0.
\]
In $\{-1,0,1\}^4$ its complete solution set is
\begin{equation}
(0,0,0,0),\qquad \pm(1,1,1,-1).
\label{eq:theta-four-tail-table}
\end{equation}
The zero tail propagates backwards to the zero word.  From a nonzero tail,
the equations $R_{m-4}=R_{m-5}=0$ give respectively
$e_{m-4}=-e_{m-3}$ and $e_{m-5}=0$; then
\eqref{eq:theta-suffix-renormalization} propagates the alternating pair
uniquely to the left.  This is precisely the recursion
\eqref{eq:theta-terminal-word}, and both signs occur.  Hence the first set
in \eqref{eq:pisot-linear-A3} is $\{\pm E_m\}$.  With one more row, the
terminal equation is
\[
e_{m-2}+2e_{m-1}+4e_m=0,
\]
whose only solution in $\{-1,0,1\}^3$ is zero.  Backward propagation then
contradicts $e_0\ne0$.  Thus
$\mathcal N_{\theta,m,\lambda_m}$ is empty.

Now let $m$ be odd and take $L=m-3=\lambda_m-1$.  The terminal five-digit
equation has, up to sign, exactly the following solutions:
\begin{equation}
(0,0,0,0,0),\quad
(1,1,1,-1,0),\quad
(1,0,1,1,-1),\quad
(1,-1,-1,-1,1).
\label{eq:theta-five-tail-table}
\end{equation}
Successive solution of $R_{m-5}=0$ gives respectively
$0,-1,0,0$ for the preceding digit.  Since $m-5$ is even,
\eqref{eq:theta-suffix-renormalization} propagates this digit to $e_0$ up
to sign.  For example, when $m=7$ the surviving tail gives
\[
(e_3,\ldots,e_7)=(1,1,1,-1,0),\qquad e_2=-1,\qquad e_0=-e_2=1.
\]
The zero tail and the last two nonzero tails instead force $e_2=0$, hence
$e_0=0$; only $\pm(1,1,1,-1,0)$ survive.  They give exactly the even
terminal word for aperture $m-1$ followed by zero, namely $\pm E_m$.
With one more row the terminal four-digit alternatives are
\eqref{eq:theta-four-tail-table}; now $m-5$ has even parity and its forced
zero propagates to $e_0$.  Hence the next obstruction set is empty.

For $m=4,5$, successive exact division in the two or three window
congruences gives
\[
\begin{array}{c|c|c|c}
m&Q_m&\mathcal N_{\theta,m,\lambda_m-1}
&\mathcal N_{\theta,m,\lambda_m}\\ \hline
4&12&\{\pm(1,-1,-1,-1,1)\}&\varnothing\\
5&21&\{\pm(1,-1,-1,-1,1,0)\}&\varnothing.
\end{array}
\]
This is an exhaustive integer calculation: in each row the quotient is in
$\{-1,0,1\}$ by \eqref{eq:theta-carry-one}, and the last digit determines
the preceding bounded digit successively.  Thus \textup{(A3)} holds for
every $m\ge4$.  Lemma~\ref{lem:pisot-exact-collision-quotient} now gives
\textup{(A2)}, including injectivity, and the limiting statement is
immediate.

Finally, the same collision rows are exactly edges of $G_m(U_\theta,1)$.
The vectors $\pm E_m$ give paths of length $\lambda_m-1$, while the empty
next obstruction set rules out any longer path or reachable cycle.  Since
$\lambda_m-1\ge m-3$, this proves the two asserted sharpness conclusions.
\end{proof}

 succeeded in 192ms:
\section{Cyclic rank recodings and the collision quotient}
\label{sec:cyclic-rank-application}

A Pisot linear numeration system is a sequence $U=(u_j)_{j\ge0}$ of positive
integers satisfying the recurrence whose characteristic polynomial is the
minimal polynomial of a Pisot number.  Its initial values need not be the
standard ones.  Here ``standard'' has its technical meaning
$u_{-1}=\cdots=u_{-d+1}=0$, $u_0=1$; we do not use it as a synonym for the
larger class considered below.  Assume that $U$ is strictly increasing and
$u_0=1$, and let
\[
D_U=\{0,\ldots,d_U\}
\]
be its canonical greedy digit set.  The canonical greedy $U$-normal words of
length $m$ are
\begin{equation}
X_{U,m}=\left\{x\in D_U^m:
 \sum_{j=0}^k x_ju_j<u_{k+1}\quad(0\le k<m)\right\}.
\label{eq:pisot-canonical-words}
\end{equation}
Consequently
\[
\Val_{U,m}(x)=\sum_{j=0}^{m-1}x_ju_j
\]
maps $X_{U,m}$ bijectively onto $\{0,\ldots,u_m-1\}$.  Define
\begin{equation}
\Fold_{U,m}(w)=
(\Val_{U,m}|_{X_{U,m}})^{-1}
       (\Val_{U,m}(w)\bmod u_m)
\label{eq:pisot-canonical-fold}
\end{equation}
and let $\Phi_{U,m}$ be the corresponding sliding code on
$D_U^{\mathbb Z}$.  This is the cyclic language-rank recoding: each output
word is the unique legal word having the cyclically reduced rank of the raw
window.

Define $\ell_{\rm cau}(U,m)$ to be the least $L\ge1$ for which the raw digit
at time zero is determined, on the image of $\Phi_{U,m}$, by the output
windows at times $0,\ldots,L-1$; set it to $\infty$ if no such $L$ exists.
Thus $\ell_{\rm cau}(U,m)-1$ is the least anticipation of an inverse
constrained to have memory zero.  Relabeling each output by its canonical
rank turns the local rule into
\[
(x_0,\ldots,x_{m-1})\longmapsto
\sum_{j=0}^{m-1}u_jx_j\pmod {u_m}.
\]
This symbolwise relabeling is bijective.  The rule is not a linear cellular
automaton: in general $D_U$ is only a digit subset of
$\mathbb Z/u_m\mathbb Z$, not a module on which the rule is a group
endomorphism.

Put $\Delta_U=[-d_U,d_U]\cap\mathbb Z$ and, for $L\ge1$, set
\begin{equation}
\mathcal N_{U,m,L}=\left\{e\in\Delta_U^{m+L-1}:
 e_0\ne0,\quad
 \sum_{j=0}^{m-1}u_je_{t+j}\equiv0\pmod {u_m}
 \ (0\le t<L)\right\}.
\label{eq:pisot-bad-sliding-set}
\end{equation}
Equivalently, $\mathcal N_{U,m,L}$ is the finite-block kernel of the
$L$-row banded Toeplitz operator with kernel
$(u_0,\ldots,u_{m-1})$ over $\mathbb Z/u_m\mathbb Z$, restricted by
$e_0\ne0$ and by the coefficient bound $\Delta_U$.  Every row has a unique
integer quotient $k_t$:
\begin{equation}
\sum_{j=0}^{m-1}u_je_{t+j}=k_tu_m.
\label{eq:pisot-window-carry}
\end{equation}
Equivalently,
\begin{equation}
[e_t,e_{t+1},\ldots,e_{t+m-1},-k_t]_U=0,
\label{eq:pisot-zero-word-bridge}
\end{equation}
where the last coordinate has weight $u_m$.  Thus every collision row is an
edge of $G_m(U,d_U)$, with completing coefficient $-k_t$.

\begin{lemma}[Exact collision quotient for $U$]
\label{lem:pisot-exact-collision-quotient}
For every $m\ge2$,
\begin{equation}
\ell_{\rm cau}(U,m)
=\min\{L\ge1:\mathcal N_{U,m,L}=\varnothing\}.
\label{eq:pisot-exact-depth}
\end{equation}
Let $\Gamma_{U,m}$ have vertex set $\Delta_U^{m-1}$ and an edge
$(v_0,\ldots,v_{m-2})\to(v_1,\ldots,v_{m-2},a)$ exactly when
\[
\sum_{j=0}^{m-2}u_jv_j+u_{m-1}a\equiv0\pmod {u_m}.
\]
If $B_{U,m}$ is the set of vertices with first coordinate nonzero, then
\begin{equation}
\ell_{\rm cau}(U,m)=1+\sup\{\text{edge length of a path from }B_{U,m}\}.
\label{eq:pisot-longest-path}
\end{equation}
Moreover, $\Phi_{U,m}$ is injective if and only if no directed cycle of
$\Gamma_{U,m}$ is reachable from $B_{U,m}$.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
Two folded windows agree exactly when the difference of their raw values is
divisible by $u_m$, because the canonical normal words represent
$0,\ldots,u_m-1$ bijectively.  Every $e\in\Delta_U$ is realized as a
difference of two digits by taking its positive and negative parts in
$D_U$.  Hence two output $L$-blocks with distinct initial raw digits are
equivalent to a vector in $\mathcal N_{U,m,L}$, with no lost collisions.
Reading consecutive difference windows gives precisely the paths from
$B_{U,m}$ in $\Gamma_{U,m}$, proving \eqref{eq:pisot-exact-depth} and
\eqref{eq:pisot-longest-path}.  A reachable nonzero cycle can be repeated
periodically and is realized by two raw points.

It remains only to note why injectivity rules out the zero loop as a
reachable cycle.  If $d_U\ge u_m$, the constant nonzero difference sequence
$e_t=u_m$ belongs to $\Delta_U^{\mathbb Z}$ and every window sum is divisible
by $u_m$; hence $\Phi_{U,m}$ is noninjective and the difference graph has a
nonzero loop.  In the only remaining case, $d_U<u_m$.  If a vertex has an
edge to the zero vertex, its overlap coordinates vanish and its first
coordinate $a$ satisfies $a\equiv0\pmod {u_m}$.  The bound
$|a|\le d_U<u_m$ gives $a=0$, so the zero vertex is its own unique
predecessor.  This proves the last assertion without imposing a restriction
on the allowed nonstandard initial values.
\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}[Aperture-linear future-only inverse depth]
\label{cor:pisot-linear-causal-depth}
Let $U$ be any strictly increasing Pisot numeration system with $u_0=1$,
canonical greedy digit set and canonical normal words.  There is an
effectively computable $C_U<\infty$ such that, for every $m\ge2$,
\begin{equation*}
\Phi_{U,m}\text{ injective}
\quad\Longrightarrow\quad
\ell_{\rm cau}(U,m)\le C_Um.
\tag{A1}\label{eq:pisot-linear-A1}
\end{equation*}
Equivalently, if no first-coordinate-nonzero state of $\Gamma_{U,m}$ can
reach a directed cycle, every directed path from that initial state set has
length at most $C_Um-1$.
\end{corollary}

\begin{proof}
If the Pisot root has degree one, then $u_j=b^j$,
$D_U=\{0,\ldots,b-1\}$, the fold is the identity, and
$\ell_{\rm cau}(U,m)=1$; take $C_U=1$.  Otherwise apply
Theorem~\ref{thm:bounded-zero-linear-transient} with $D=d_U$.  Its graph is
exactly $\Gamma_{U,m}$, because \eqref{eq:pisot-zero-word-bridge} is the
edge condition \eqref{eq:bounded-zero-overlap-edge-theorem}.  By Lemma
\ref{lem:pisot-exact-collision-quotient}, injectivity is equivalent to the
absence of a reachable cycle and the causal length is one plus the longest
path length.  Since the theorem gives fewer than $C(U,d_U)m$ edges, the
integer path length is at most $C(U,d_U)m-1$.  Taking
$C_U=C(U,d_U)$ proves both formulations.
\end{proof}

The scope is not a disguised standard-initial-value convention.  For
example, standard $k$-bonacci initial conditions begin $1,1,\ldots$ and fail
strict increase, whereas the customary shift beginning $1,2,\ldots$ belongs
to the present class.  Nor is Condition~F assumed: the principal root of
$x^3-3x^2+2x-1$ is Pisot but does not satisfy Condition~F
\cite[Theorem~3]{Akiyama2000}.  The equivalence of Condition~F with zero
preservation is proved in \cite{CartonSudberyYassawi2026}, but neither
property enters the corollary.

 succeeded in 221ms:
   1: \section{One-position overlap chains of bounded zero representations}
   2: \label{sec:bounded-zero-transients}
   3: 
   4: Let $U=(u_n)_{n\ge0}$ be a strictly increasing sequence of positive
   5: integers with $u_0=1$.  We assume that $U$ satisfies the integral recurrence
   6: whose characteristic polynomial is the minimal polynomial of a nonintegral
   7: Pisot number $\beta$.  Thus, if
   8: $\beta=\beta_0,\beta_1,\ldots,\beta_{s-1}$ are the roots of that polynomial,
   9: then $\beta>1$ and $|\beta_i|<1$ for $i\ge1$.  The recurrence and the initial
  10: values effectively give algebraic numbers $c_i$ such that
  11: \begin{equation}
  12: u_n=\sum_{i=0}^{s-1}c_i\beta_i^n,
  13: \qquad c_0>0.
  14: \label{eq:pisot-binet}
  15: \end{equation}
  16: Effective root isolation gives positive algebraic bounds $b_U,A_U$ with
  17: \begin{equation}
  18: b_U\beta^n\le u_n\le A_U\beta^n\qquad(n\ge0).
  19: \label{eq:pisot-effective-growth}
  20: \end{equation}
  21: Indeed, one isolates an index after which the total conjugate contribution
  22: in \eqref{eq:pisot-binet} is at most $c_0\beta^n/2$ and inspects the
  23: remaining finite prefix.
  24: 
  25: Fix $D\ge1$.  For $m\ge2$, let $G_m(U,D)$ be the directed graph with vertex
  26: set
  27: \[
  28: ([-D,D]\cap\mathbb Z)^{m-1}.
  29: \]
  30: There is an edge
  31: \[
  32: (e_0,\ldots,e_{m-2})\longrightarrow
  33: (e_1,\ldots,e_{m-2},e_{m-1})
  34: \]
  35: if and only if there is an integer $c$ such that
  36: \begin{equation}
  37: e_0u_0+e_1u_1+\cdots+e_{m-1}u_{m-1}+cu_m=0.
  38: \label{eq:bounded-zero-overlap-edge}
  39: \end{equation}
  40: An edge is therefore a bounded $U$-representation of zero, organized so
  41: that consecutive representations overlap in one position.  A path is a
  42: one-position overlap chain.  We distinguish the initial vertices whose
  43: first coordinate is nonzero because an arbitrary nonzero chain can be
  44: shifted until its first exposed nonzero coefficient occupies that position.
  45: 
  46: Put
  47: \begin{equation}
  48: K_{U,D}=\left\lceil\frac{DA_U}{b_U(\beta-1)}\right\rceil.
  49: \label{eq:bounded-zero-carry-bound}
  50: \end{equation}
  51: The coefficient $c$ completing an edge is unique and satisfies
  52: $|c|\le K_{U,D}$: uniqueness follows from $u_m>0$, and
  53: \[
  54: |c|\le D\frac{\sum_{j<m}u_j}{u_m}
  55:  <\frac{DA_U}{b_U(\beta-1)}.
  56: \]
  57: The proof compares adjacent edges through all embeddings of the Pisot field.
  58: The following constants bound the conjugate contributions and separate the
  59: finitely many possible dominant-embedding defects from zero.
  60: Define the effective positive separation
  61: \begin{equation}
  62: \delta_{U,D}=\min\left\{|a+k-\beta l|:
  63:  |a|\le D,\ |k|,|l|\le K_{U,D},\ a+k-\beta l\ne0\right\}.
  64: \label{eq:bounded-zero-separation}
  65: \end{equation}
  66: The variables in this minimum are integers.  Since $\beta$ is irrational,
  67: $\delta_{U,D}>0$, and exact algebraic-number comparison computes it.  Set
  68: \begin{align}
  69: M_{U,D}&=\frac1{c_0}\sum_{i=1}^{s-1}|c_i|
  70:  \left(\frac{D}{1-|\beta_i|}+K_{U,D}\right),\nonumber\\
  71: B_{U,D}&=D+(1+\beta)M_{U,D},
  72: \label{eq:bounded-zero-conjugate-bound}
  73: \end{align}
  74: and
  75: \begin{equation}
  76: m_0(U,D)=\min\{m\ge2:\beta^m\delta_{U,D}>B_{U,D}\}.
  77: \label{eq:bounded-zero-contraction-threshold}
  78: \end{equation}
  79: All these constants are effective from $U$ and $D$.
  80: 
  81: \begin{lemma}[Adjacent collapse for an arbitrary bounded coefficient alphabet]
  82: \label{lem:bounded-zero-arbitrary-alphabet-collapse}
  83: Let $U$ satisfy the integral recurrence whose characteristic polynomial is
  84: the minimal polynomial of a nonintegral Pisot number $\beta$, and fix
  85: $D\ge1$.  If $m\ge m_0(U,D)$, coefficients
  86: $e_t,\ldots,e_{t+m}\in[-D,D]\cap\mathbb Z$, and integers $k_t,k_{t+1}$
  87: satisfy
  88: \[
  89: \sum_{j=0}^{m-1}u_je_{t+j}=k_tu_m,
  90: \qquad
  91: \sum_{j=0}^{m-1}u_je_{t+1+j}=k_{t+1}u_m,
  92: \]
  93: then
  94: \[
  95: k_{t+1}=0,\qquad e_{t+m}=-k_t.
  96: \]
  97: \end{lemma}
  98: 
  99: \begin{proof}
 100: The growth bounds give, for either row,
 101: \[
 102: |k_t|\le D\frac{\sum_{j<m}u_j}{u_m}
 103:  <\frac{DA_U}{b_U(\beta-1)}\le K_{U,D},
 104: \]
 105: and similarly $|k_{t+1}|\le K_{U,D}$.  For every embedding set
 106: \[
 107: A_{t,i}=\sum_{j=0}^{m-1}e_{t+j}\beta_i^j-k_t\beta_i^m.
 108: \]
 109: The recurrence and its Binet expansion give $\sum_i c_iA_{t,i}=0$.
 110: For $i\ge1$,
 111: \[
 112: |A_{t,i}|\le\frac{D}{1-|\beta_i|}+K_{U,D},
 113: \]
 114: so $|A_{t,0}|\le M_{U,D}$, and the same holds at $t+1$.
 115: Direct subtraction of the adjacent dominant-embedding sums gives the exact
 116: identity
 117: \begin{equation}
 118: \beta^m(e_{t+m}+k_t-\beta k_{t+1})
 119: =\beta A_{t+1,0}-A_{t,0}+e_t.
 120: \label{eq:bounded-zero-adjacent-identity}
 121: \end{equation}
 122: The right side has modulus at most $B_{U,D}$.  If the parenthesis on the
 123: left were nonzero, \eqref{eq:bounded-zero-separation} and
 124: \eqref{eq:bounded-zero-contraction-threshold} would make the left side
 125: strictly larger than $B_{U,D}$, a contradiction.  Hence
 126: $e_{t+m}+k_t=\beta k_{t+1}$.  Irrationality of $\beta$ and integrality of
 127: the three coefficients force $k_{t+1}=0$ and $e_{t+m}=-k_t$.
 128: \end{proof}
 129: 
 130: \begin{theorem}[Linear transient bound for bounded $U$-representations of zero]
 131: \label{thm:bounded-zero-linear-transient}
 132: Let $U=(u_n)_{n\ge0}$ be a strictly increasing sequence of positive integers
 133: with $u_0=1$, satisfying the integral recurrence whose characteristic
 134: polynomial is the minimal polynomial of a nonintegral Pisot number $\beta$.
 135: Fix $D\ge1$, and let $G_m(U,D)$ be the graph defined above.  For every edge,
 136: the completing coefficient is unique and necessarily satisfies
 137: $|c|\le K_{U,D}$.
 138: Then there is an effectively computable $C(U,D)<\infty$ such that, for every
 139: $m\ge2$, if no directed cycle of $G_m(U,D)$ is reachable from a vertex whose
 140: first coordinate is nonzero, every directed path beginning at such a vertex
 141: has fewer than $C(U,D)m$ edges.
 142: 
 143: Equivalently, after shifting a chain so that its initial exposed coefficient
 144: is nonzero, either it reaches a directed cycle, or it has fewer than
 145: $C(U,D)m$ further overlaps.  The graphs are finite-state recognizers of these
 146: overlap chains, and their edges are bounded $U$-representations of zero by
 147: \eqref{eq:bounded-zero-overlap-edge}.
 148: \end{theorem}
 149: 
 150: \begin{proof}
 151: For an edge at time $t$, write $k_t=-c$ in
 152: \eqref{eq:bounded-zero-overlap-edge}.  The growth estimate gives
 153: $|k_t|\le K_{U,D}$ uniformly in $m$.  Suppose first that
 154: $m\ge m_0(U,D)$.  Along any path of at least two edges, Lemma
 155: \ref{lem:bounded-zero-arbitrary-alphabet-collapse}, applied to each adjacent
 156: pair, gives
 157: \[
 158: k_1=k_2=\cdots=0,\qquad
 159: e_m=-k_0,\qquad e_{m+1}=e_{m+2}=\cdots=0.
 160: \]
 161: A path of $m+1$ edges therefore reaches the all-zero vertex.  That vertex
 162: has a loop, so such a path would make a directed cycle reachable from its
 163: initial vertex.  Under the hypothesis of the theorem, every path has at
 164: most $m$ edges for $m\ge m_0(U,D)$.
 165: 
 166: For each of the finitely many $2\le m<m_0(U,D)$, the graph is finite.  When
 167: no cycle is reachable from the first-coordinate-nonzero vertices, exact
 168: acyclic longest-path search computes the maximum $p_{U,D}(m)$; set it to
 169: zero otherwise.  Thus, for example,
 170: \[
 171: C(U,D)=\max\left(2,
 172:  \max_{2\le m<m_0(U,D)}
 173:  \left\lceil\frac{1+p_{U,D}(m)}m\right\rceil\right)
 174: \]
 175: is effective and has the required property, with the inner maximum understood
 176: as zero when $m_0(U,D)=2$.
 177: \end{proof}
 178: 
 179: \begin{corollary}[Global chain dichotomy]
 180: \label{cor:bounded-zero-global-dichotomy}
 181: Under the hypotheses of Theorem~\ref{thm:bounded-zero-linear-transient}, fix
 182: $m\ge2$ and consider all bounded zero words of length $m+1$ that overlap one
 183: digit at a time.  Either some chain with a nonzero coefficient has a finite
 184: prefix followed by a periodic bounded zero representation, or every nonzero
 185: chain has fewer than $(C(U,D)+1)m$ rows.
 186: \end{corollary}
 187: 
 188: \begin{proof}
 189: A reachable cycle gives the periodic tail.  In the absence of such a cycle,
 190: at most $m-1$ initial shifts pass before a nonzero coordinate occupies the
 191: first position, after which Theorem~\ref{thm:bounded-zero-linear-transient}
 192: bounds the remaining overlaps.  The alternatives are mutually exclusive
 193: because a periodic tail can be repeated to make arbitrarily long chains.
 194: \end{proof}
 195: 
 196: \begin{remark}[Arithmetic scope]
 197: \label{rem:bounded-zero-standard-object-boundary}
 198: Theorem~\ref{thm:bounded-zero-linear-transient} is independent of the cyclic
 199: rank construction of Section~\ref{sec:cyclic-rank-application}.  Its
 200: essential arithmetic features are the distinguished terminal weight $u_m$
 201: and the use of consecutive recurrence weights; replacing $u_m$ by an
 202: unrelated modulus removes the identity underlying Lemma
 203: \ref{lem:bounded-zero-arbitrary-alphabet-collapse}.  These features are what
 204: make the linear estimate available for the overlap graphs considered here.
 205: \end{remark}

 succeeded in 213ms:
   1: \section{A fixed cubic system with exact linear depth}
   2: \label{sec:fixed-cubic-sharpness}
   3: 
   4: Let $\theta$ be the real root of $x^3-2x^2+x-1$ and define
   5: $U_\theta=(Q_j(\theta))_{j\ge0}$ by
   6: \begin{equation}
   7: Q_0(\theta)=1,\quad Q_1(\theta)=2,\quad Q_2(\theta)=4,
   8: \quad Q_{j+3}(\theta)=2Q_{j+2}(\theta)-Q_{j+1}(\theta)+Q_j(\theta).
   9: \label{eq:theta-count-recurrence}
  10: \end{equation}
  11: The canonical digit and difference alphabets will be $\{0,1\}$ and
  12: $\{-1,0,1\}$, respectively.  For $m\ge4$ put
  13: \[
  14: \lambda_m=2\left\lfloor\frac m2\right\rfloor-1
  15: \]
  16: and define the signed terminal words by
  17: \begin{equation}
  18: \begin{gathered}
  19: E_4=(1,-1,-1,-1,1),\\
  20: E_m=(1,0,-E_{m-2},0,0)\quad(m\ge6\text{ even}),\\
  21: E_m=(E_{m-1},0)\quad(m\ge5\text{ odd}).
  22: \end{gathered}
  23: \label{eq:theta-terminal-word}
  24: \end{equation}
  25: Here juxtaposition denotes concatenation and a prefixed minus changes every
  26: sign.
  27: 
  28: \begin{theorem}[Fixed cubic sharpness and terminal classification]
  29: \label{thm:fixed-cubic-sharpness}
  30: For every $m\ge4$,
  31: \begin{equation*}
  32: \Phi_{\theta,m}\text{ is injective},\qquad
  33: \ell_{\rm cau}(\theta,m)=2\left\lfloor\frac m2\right\rfloor-1.
  34: \tag{A2}\label{eq:pisot-linear-A2}
  35: \end{equation*}
  36: More precisely,
  37: \begin{equation*}
  38: \mathcal N_{\theta,m,\lambda_m-1}=\{E_m,-E_m\}\ne\varnothing,
  39: \qquad
  40: \mathcal N_{\theta,m,\lambda_m}=\varnothing.
  41: \tag{A3}\label{eq:pisot-linear-A3}
  42: \end{equation*}
  43: Consequently
  44: \[
  45: \lim_{m\to\infty}\frac{\ell_{\rm cau}(\theta,m)}m=1.
  46: \]
  47: Moreover, in the bounded-zero graph $G_m(U_\theta,1)$ the maximum path
  48: length from a first-coordinate-nonzero vertex is $\lambda_m-1$, hence at
  49: least $m-3$.  Thus no uniform theorem for the fixed systems covered by
  50: \textup{(A1)}, and no uniform analogue of
  51: Theorem~\ref{thm:bounded-zero-linear-transient}, can replace the linear order
  52: by $o(m)$.
  53: \end{theorem}
  54: 
  55: \begin{remark}[Simple-Parry input]
  56: The identity $d_\theta(1)=1101\,0^\infty$ supplies the legal language and its
  57: rank recurrence.  After that identification, the proof uses the recurrence
  58: for $Q_j(\theta)$, a direct quotient bound, the contracting-conjugate
  59: estimate, the suffix-sum identity, and the terminal integer equations.
  60: \end{remark}
  61: 
  62: \begin{remark}[Order sharpness]
  63: Sharpness here concerns growth order for the fixed recurrence $U_\theta$ and
  64: the fixed coefficient alphabet $\{-1,0,1\}$.  The effective constants in
  65: Theorem~\ref{thm:bounded-zero-linear-transient} and Corollary
  66: \ref{cor:pisot-linear-causal-depth} are not claimed to be numerically
  67: optimal across systems.
  68: \end{remark}
  69: 
  70: \begin{proof}
  71: Writing $f(x)=x^3-2x^2+x-1$, we have
  72: $f(7/4)=-1/64$ and $f(351/200)=3151/8000000$.  The critical points of $f$
  73: are $1/3$ and $1$, and $f$ is negative at both, so these endpoint values
  74: isolate its only real zero.  The product of the two nonreal roots has modulus
  75: $1/\theta$, and hence $\theta$ is Pisot.  Its defining equation gives
  76: \[
  77: 1=\theta^{-1}+\theta^{-2}+\theta^{-4},
  78: \]
  79: and every proper suffix of $1101\,0^\infty$ is lexicographically smaller.
  80: Parry's criterion therefore gives $d_\theta(1)=1101\,0^\infty$, and the
  81: canonical language counts are exactly \eqref{eq:theta-count-recurrence}.
  82: In particular, the digit and difference alphabets are $\{0,1\}$ and
  83: $\{-1,0,1\}$.
  84: 
  85: The recurrence also implies the positive recurrence
  86: $Q_{n+4}=Q_{n+3}+Q_{n+2}+Q_n$.  Thus
  87: $2Q_{n+1}-3Q_n>0$ follows from its four positive initial values.  Starting
  88: with $Q_0<2Q_1$ and using
  89: $\sum_{j<m+1}Q_j<3Q_m<2Q_{m+1}$ then gives
  90: \begin{equation}
  91: \sum_{j<m}Q_j(\theta)<2Q_m(\theta).
  92: \label{eq:theta-carry-one}
  93: \end{equation}
  94: Every quotient in \eqref{eq:pisot-window-carry} therefore belongs to
  95: $\{-1,0,1\}$.
  96: 
  97: We verify the contraction threshold explicitly.  If
  98: $\rho,\overline\rho$ are the nonreal roots, write
  99: \[
 100: Q_n=c_\theta\theta^n+c_\rho\rho^n+
 101: \overline{c_\rho}\,\overline\rho^n.
 102: \]
 103: The rational isolating interval above and the residue formula for
 104: \[
 105: \sum_{n\ge0}Q_nz^n=\frac{1+z^2}{1-2z+z^2-z^3}
 106: \]
 107: give
 108: \[
 109: 1.26<c_\theta<1.27,\qquad |c_\rho|<0.19,
 110: \qquad |\rho|<0.756.
 111: \]
 112: Here $c_\alpha=(\alpha^2+1)/(3\alpha^2-4\alpha+1)$ for every root
 113: $\alpha$, and $|\rho|=\theta^{-1/2}$.  All displayed decimals are terminating
 114: rational bounds obtained by substitution of the endpoints $7/4$ and
 115: $351/200$ and exact rational arithmetic.  The dominant-embedding error in
 116: the proof of Lemma~\ref{lem:bounded-zero-arbitrary-alphabet-collapse} is
 117: therefore less than $1.54$.  For the actual quotient set
 118: $\{-1,0,1\}$, the nonzero separation in
 119: $e_{t+m}+k_t-\theta k_{t+1}$ is at least
 120: $\delta_\theta=2-\theta>0.245$, and
 121: \[
 122: \theta^6\delta_\theta>7.03
 123: >1+(1+\theta)1.54.
 124: \]
 125: Repeating the exact identity
 126: \eqref{eq:bounded-zero-adjacent-identity} with this sharpened finite quotient
 127: set gives
 128: \begin{equation}
 129: k_{t+1}=0,\qquad e_{t+m}=-k_t
 130: \label{eq:theta-carry-collapse}
 131: \end{equation}
 132: for every adjacent pair of collision rows and every $m\ge6$.  The apertures
 133: $m=4,5$ are included in the exact terminal check below.
 134: 
 135: It remains to classify the longest paths.  Fix $m\ge6$ and a vector with at
 136: least two collision rows.  After \eqref{eq:theta-carry-collapse}, all its
 137: coordinates beyond $m$ vanish except possibly $e_m=-k_0$.  Define the full
 138: suffix sums
 139: \[
 140: R_t=\sum_{j\ge0}Q_j(\theta)e_{t+j}.
 141: \]
 142: Every collision row with $t\ge1$ is now $R_t=0$, and the first row together
 143: with $e_m=-k_0$ gives $R_0=0$.  The recurrence implies the exact identity
 144: \begin{equation}
 145: R_t=e_t+e_{t+2}+2R_{t+1}-R_{t+2}+R_{t+3}.
 146: \label{eq:theta-suffix-renormalization}
 147: \end{equation}
 148: Thus four consecutive zero suffix sums force $e_t=-e_{t+2}$.
 149: 
 150: Let first $m$ be even and take $L=m-2=\lambda_m-1$.  We have
 151: $R_t=0$ for $0\le t\le m-3$.  The terminal equation is
 152: \[
 153: e_{m-3}+2e_{m-2}+4e_{m-1}+7e_m=0.
 154: \]
 155: In $\{-1,0,1\}^4$ its complete solution set is
 156: \begin{equation}
 157: (0,0,0,0),\qquad \pm(1,1,1,-1).
 158: \label{eq:theta-four-tail-table}
 159: \end{equation}
 160: The zero tail propagates backwards to the zero word.  From a nonzero tail,
 161: the equations $R_{m-4}=R_{m-5}=0$ give respectively
 162: $e_{m-4}=-e_{m-3}$ and $e_{m-5}=0$; then
 163: \eqref{eq:theta-suffix-renormalization} propagates the alternating pair
 164: uniquely to the left.  This is precisely the recursion
 165: \eqref{eq:theta-terminal-word}, and both signs occur.  Hence the first set
 166: in \eqref{eq:pisot-linear-A3} is $\{\pm E_m\}$.  With one more row, the
 167: terminal equation is
 168: \[
 169: e_{m-2}+2e_{m-1}+4e_m=0,
 170: \]
 171: whose only solution in $\{-1,0,1\}^3$ is zero.  Backward propagation then
 172: contradicts $e_0\ne0$.  Thus
 173: $\mathcal N_{\theta,m,\lambda_m}$ is empty.
 174: 
 175: Now let $m$ be odd and take $L=m-3=\lambda_m-1$.  The terminal five-digit
 176: equation has, up to sign, exactly the following solutions:
 177: \begin{equation}
 178: (0,0,0,0,0),\quad
 179: (1,1,1,-1,0),\quad
 180: (1,0,1,1,-1),\quad
 181: (1,-1,-1,-1,1).
 182: \label{eq:theta-five-tail-table}
 183: \end{equation}
 184: Successive solution of $R_{m-5}=0$ gives respectively
 185: $0,-1,0,0$ for the preceding digit.  Since $m-5$ is even,
 186: \eqref{eq:theta-suffix-renormalization} propagates this digit to $e_0$ up
 187: to sign.  For example, when $m=7$ the surviving tail gives
 188: \[
 189: (e_3,\ldots,e_7)=(1,1,1,-1,0),\qquad e_2=-1,\qquad e_0=-e_2=1.
 190: \]
 191: The zero tail and the last two nonzero tails instead force $e_2=0$, hence
 192: $e_0=0$; only $\pm(1,1,1,-1,0)$ survive.  They give exactly the even
 193: terminal word for aperture $m-1$ followed by zero, namely $\pm E_m$.
 194: With one more row the terminal four-digit alternatives are
 195: \eqref{eq:theta-four-tail-table}; now $m-5$ has even parity and its forced
 196: zero propagates to $e_0$.  Hence the next obstruction set is empty.
 197: 
 198: For $m=4,5$, successive exact division in the two or three window
 199: congruences gives
 200: \[
 201: \begin{array}{c|c|c|c}
 202: m&Q_m&\mathcal N_{\theta,m,\lambda_m-1}
 203: &\mathcal N_{\theta,m,\lambda_m}\\ \hline
 204: 4&12&\{\pm(1,-1,-1,-1,1)\}&\varnothing\\
 205: 5&21&\{\pm(1,-1,-1,-1,1,0)\}&\varnothing.
 206: \end{array}
 207: \]
 208: This is an exhaustive integer calculation: in each row the quotient is in
 209: $\{-1,0,1\}$ by \eqref{eq:theta-carry-one}, and the last digit determines
 210: the preceding bounded digit successively.  Thus \textup{(A3)} holds for
 211: every $m\ge4$.  Lemma~\ref{lem:pisot-exact-collision-quotient} now gives
 212: \textup{(A2)}, including injectivity, and the limiting statement is
 213: immediate.
 214: 
 215: Finally, the same collision rows are exactly edges of $G_m(U_\theta,1)$.
 216: The vectors $\pm E_m$ give paths of length $\lambda_m-1$, while the empty
 217: next obstruction set rules out any longer path or reachable cycle.  Since
 218: $\lambda_m-1\ge m-3$, this proves the two asserted sharpness conclusions.
 219: \end{proof}

 \section{Cyclic rank recodings and the collision quotient}
 \label{sec:cyclic-rank-application}
 
+We now code the full shift on the canonical digit alphabet by replacing each
+raw length-$m$ window with the legal word of the same cyclic rank.  A
+future-only inverse recovers the raw symbol at time zero from output windows
+beginning at time zero, without using past output.  Equality of two folded
+windows says precisely that their raw ranks are congruent modulo $u_m$.
+Taking coordinatewise differences converts a run of such congruences into
+overlapping bounded representations of zero.  The exactness of this quotient
+is crucial: it identifies all collisions, so path length in the arithmetic
+graph measures inverse depth rather than merely bounding it.
+
 A Pisot linear numeration system is a sequence $U=(u_j)_{j\ge0}$ of positive
 integers satisfying the recurrence whose characteristic polynomial is the
-minimal polynomial of a Pisot number.  Its initial values need not be the
-standard ones.  Here ``standard'' has its technical meaning
-$u_{-1}=\cdots=u_{-d+1}=0$, $u_0=1$; we do not use it as a synonym for the
-larger class considered below.  Assume that $U$ is strictly increasing and
-$u_0=1$, and let
+minimal polynomial of a Pisot number.  We allow nonstandard initial values
+and assume that $U$ is strictly increasing with $u_0=1$.  Let
 \[
 D_U=\{0,\ldots,d_U\}
 \]
 be its canonical greedy digit set.  The canonical greedy $U$-normal words of
 length $m$ are
 \begin{equation}
 X_{U,m}=\left\{x\in D_U^m:
  \sum_{j=0}^k x_ju_j<u_{k+1}\quad(0\le k<m)\right\}.
 \label{eq:pisot-canonical-words}
 \end{equation}
 Consequently
 \[
 \Val_{U,m}(x)=\sum_{j=0}^{m-1}x_ju_j
 \]
 maps $X_{U,m}$ bijectively onto $\{0,\ldots,u_m-1\}$.  Define
 \begin{equation}
 \Fold_{U,m}(w)=
 (\Val_{U,m}|_{X_{U,m}})^{-1}
        (\Val_{U,m}(w)\bmod u_m)
 \label{eq:pisot-canonical-fold}
 \end{equation}
 and let $\Phi_{U,m}$ be the corresponding sliding code on
 $D_U^{\mathbb Z}$.  This is the cyclic language-rank recoding: each output
 word is the unique legal word having the cyclically reduced rank of the raw
 window.
 
 Define $\ell_{\rm cau}(U,m)$ to be the least $L\ge1$ for which the raw digit
 at time zero is determined, on the image of $\Phi_{U,m}$, by the output
 windows at times $0,\ldots,L-1$; set it to $\infty$ if no such $L$ exists.
 Thus $\ell_{\rm cau}(U,m)-1$ is the least anticipation of an inverse
 constrained to have memory zero.  Relabeling each output by its canonical
 rank turns the local rule into
 \[
 (x_0,\ldots,x_{m-1})\longmapsto
 \sum_{j=0}^{m-1}u_jx_j\pmod {u_m}.
 \]
-This symbolwise relabeling is bijective.  The rule is not a linear cellular
-automaton: in general $D_U$ is only a digit subset of
-$\mathbb Z/u_m\mathbb Z$, not a module on which the rule is a group
-endomorphism.
+This symbolwise relabeling is bijective.
 
 Put $\Delta_U=[-d_U,d_U]\cap\mathbb Z$ and, for $L\ge1$, set
 \begin{equation}
 \mathcal N_{U,m,L}=\left\{e\in\Delta_U^{m+L-1}:
  e_0\ne0,\quad
  \sum_{j=0}^{m-1}u_je_{t+j}\equiv0\pmod {u_m}
  \ (0\le t<L)\right\}.
 \label{eq:pisot-bad-sliding-set}
 \end{equation}
 Equivalently, $\mathcal N_{U,m,L}$ is the finite-block kernel of the
 $L$-row banded Toeplitz operator with kernel
 $(u_0,\ldots,u_{m-1})$ over $\mathbb Z/u_m\mathbb Z$, restricted by
 $e_0\ne0$ and by the coefficient bound $\Delta_U$.  Every row has a unique
 integer quotient $k_t$:
 \begin{equation}
 \sum_{j=0}^{m-1}u_je_{t+j}=k_tu_m.
 \label{eq:pisot-window-carry}
 \end{equation}
 Equivalently,
 \begin{equation}
 [e_t,e_{t+1},\ldots,e_{t+m-1},-k_t]_U=0,
 \label{eq:pisot-zero-word-bridge}
 \end{equation}
 where the last coordinate has weight $u_m$.  Thus every collision row is an
 edge of $G_m(U,d_U)$, with completing coefficient $-k_t$.
 
 \begin{lemma}[Exact collision quotient for $U$]
 \label{lem:pisot-exact-collision-quotient}
 For every $m\ge2$,
 \begin{equation}
 \ell_{\rm cau}(U,m)
 =\min\{L\ge1:\mathcal N_{U,m,L}=\varnothing\}.
 \label{eq:pisot-exact-depth}
 \end{equation}
 Let $\Gamma_{U,m}$ have vertex set $\Delta_U^{m-1}$ and an edge
 $(v_0,\ldots,v_{m-2})\to(v_1,\ldots,v_{m-2},a)$ exactly when
 \[
 \sum_{j=0}^{m-2}u_jv_j+u_{m-1}a\equiv0\pmod {u_m}.
 \]
 If $B_{U,m}$ is the set of vertices with first coordinate nonzero, then
 \begin{equation}
 \ell_{\rm cau}(U,m)=1+\sup\{\text{edge length of a path from }B_{U,m}\}.
 \label{eq:pisot-longest-path}
 \end{equation}
 Moreover, $\Phi_{U,m}$ is injective if and only if no directed cycle of
 $\Gamma_{U,m}$ is reachable from $B_{U,m}$.
 \end{lemma}
 
 \begin{proof}
 Two folded windows agree exactly when the difference of their raw values is
 divisible by $u_m$, because the canonical normal words represent
 $0,\ldots,u_m-1$ bijectively.  Every $e\in\Delta_U$ is realized as a
 difference of two digits by taking its positive and negative parts in
 $D_U$.  Hence two output $L$-blocks with distinct initial raw digits are
 equivalent to a vector in $\mathcal N_{U,m,L}$, with no lost collisions.
 Reading consecutive difference windows gives precisely the paths from
 $B_{U,m}$ in $\Gamma_{U,m}$, proving \eqref{eq:pisot-exact-depth} and
 \eqref{eq:pisot-longest-path}.  A reachable nonzero cycle can be repeated
 periodically and is realized by two raw points.
 
 It remains only to note why injectivity rules out the zero loop as a
 reachable cycle.  If $d_U\ge u_m$, the constant nonzero difference sequence
 $e_t=u_m$ belongs to $\Delta_U^{\mathbb Z}$ and every window sum is divisible
 by $u_m$; hence $\Phi_{U,m}$ is noninjective and the difference graph has a
 nonzero loop.  In the only remaining case, $d_U<u_m$.  If a vertex has an
 edge to the zero vertex, its overlap coordinates vanish and its first
 coordinate $a$ satisfies $a\equiv0\pmod {u_m}$.  The bound
 $|a|\le d_U<u_m$ gives $a=0$, so the zero vertex is its own unique
 predecessor.  This proves the last assertion without imposing a restriction
 on the allowed nonstandard initial values.
 \end{proof}
 
 \begin{corollary}[Aperture-linear future-only inverse depth]
 \label{cor:pisot-linear-causal-depth}
 Let $U$ be any strictly increasing Pisot numeration system with $u_0=1$,
 canonical greedy digit set and canonical normal words.  There is an
 effectively computable $C_U<\infty$ such that, for every $m\ge2$,
 \begin{equation*}
 \Phi_{U,m}\text{ injective}
 \quad\Longrightarrow\quad
 \ell_{\rm cau}(U,m)\le C_Um.
 \tag{A1}\label{eq:pisot-linear-A1}
 \end{equation*}
 Equivalently, if no first-coordinate-nonzero state of $\Gamma_{U,m}$ can
 reach a directed cycle, every directed path from that initial state set has
 length at most $C_Um-1$.
 \end{corollary}
 
 \begin{proof}
 If the Pisot root has degree one, then $u_j=b^j$,
 $D_U=\{0,\ldots,b-1\}$, the fold is the identity, and
 $\ell_{\rm cau}(U,m)=1$; take $C_U=1$.  Otherwise apply
 Theorem~\ref{thm:bounded-zero-linear-transient} with $D=d_U$.  Its graph is
 exactly $\Gamma_{U,m}$, because \eqref{eq:pisot-zero-word-bridge} is the
-edge condition \eqref{eq:bounded-zero-overlap-edge-theorem}.  By Lemma
+edge condition \eqref{eq:bounded-zero-overlap-edge}.  By Lemma
 \ref{lem:pisot-exact-collision-quotient}, injectivity is equivalent to the
 absence of a reachable cycle and the causal length is one plus the longest
 path length.  Since the theorem gives fewer than $C(U,d_U)m$ edges, the
 integer path length is at most $C(U,d_U)m-1$.  Taking
 $C_U=C(U,d_U)$ proves both formulations.
 \end{proof}
