 \section{One-position overlap chains of bounded zero representations}
 \label{sec:bounded-zero-transients}
 
 Let $U=(u_n)_{n\ge0}$ be a strictly increasing sequence of positive
 integers with $u_0=1$.  We assume that $U$ satisfies the integral recurrence
 whose characteristic polynomial is the minimal polynomial of a nonintegral
 Pisot number $\beta$.  Thus, if
 $\beta=\beta_0,\beta_1,\ldots,\beta_{s-1}$ are the roots of that polynomial,
 then $\beta>1$ and $|\beta_i|<1$ for $i\ge1$.  The recurrence and the initial
 values effectively give algebraic numbers $c_i$ such that
 \begin{equation}
 u_n=\sum_{i=0}^{s-1}c_i\beta_i^n,
 \qquad c_0>0.
 \label{eq:pisot-binet}
 \end{equation}
 Effective root isolation gives positive algebraic bounds $b_U,A_U$ with
 \begin{equation}
 b_U\beta^n\le u_n\le A_U\beta^n\qquad(n\ge0).
 \label{eq:pisot-effective-growth}
 \end{equation}
 Indeed, one isolates an index after which the total conjugate contribution
 in \eqref{eq:pisot-binet} is at most $c_0\beta^n/2$ and inspects the
 remaining finite prefix.
 
 Fix $D\ge1$.  For $m\ge2$, let $G_m(U,D)$ be the directed graph with vertex
 set
 \[
 ([-D,D]\cap\mathbb Z)^{m-1}.
 \]
 There is an edge
 \[
 (e_0,\ldots,e_{m-2})\longrightarrow
 (e_1,\ldots,e_{m-2},e_{m-1})
 \]
 if and only if there is an integer $c$ such that
 \begin{equation}
 e_0u_0+e_1u_1+\cdots+e_{m-1}u_{m-1}+cu_m=0.
 \label{eq:bounded-zero-overlap-edge}
 \end{equation}
 An edge is therefore a bounded $U$-representation of zero, organized so
 that consecutive representations overlap in one position.  A path is a
 one-position overlap chain.  We distinguish the initial vertices whose
 first coordinate is nonzero because an arbitrary nonzero chain can be
 shifted until its first exposed nonzero coefficient occupies that position.
 
 Put
 \begin{equation}
 K_{U,D}=\left\lceil\frac{DA_U}{b_U(\beta-1)}\right\rceil.
 \label{eq:bounded-zero-carry-bound}
 \end{equation}
 The coefficient $c$ completing an edge is unique and satisfies
 $|c|\le K_{U,D}$: uniqueness follows from $u_m>0$, and
 \[
 |c|\le D\frac{\sum_{j<m}u_j}{u_m}
  <\frac{DA_U}{b_U(\beta-1)}.
 \]
+The proof compares adjacent edges through all embeddings of the Pisot field.
+The following constants bound the conjugate contributions and separate the
+finitely many possible dominant-embedding defects from zero.
 Define the effective positive separation
 \begin{equation}
 \delta_{U,D}=\min\left\{|a+k-\beta l|:
  |a|\le D,\ |k|,|l|\le K_{U,D},\ a+k-\beta l\ne0\right\}.
 \label{eq:bounded-zero-separation}
 \end{equation}
 The variables in this minimum are integers.  Since $\beta$ is irrational,
 $\delta_{U,D}>0$, and exact algebraic-number comparison computes it.  Set
 \begin{align}
 M_{U,D}&=\frac1{c_0}\sum_{i=1}^{s-1}|c_i|
  \left(\frac{D}{1-|\beta_i|}+K_{U,D}\right),\nonumber\\
 B_{U,D}&=D+(1+\beta)M_{U,D},
 \label{eq:bounded-zero-conjugate-bound}
 \end{align}
 and
 \begin{equation}
 m_0(U,D)=\min\{m\ge2:\beta^m\delta_{U,D}>B_{U,D}\}.
 \label{eq:bounded-zero-contraction-threshold}
 \end{equation}
 All these constants are effective from $U$ and $D$.
 
 \begin{lemma}[Adjacent collapse for an arbitrary bounded coefficient alphabet]
 \label{lem:bounded-zero-arbitrary-alphabet-collapse}
 Let $U$ satisfy the integral recurrence whose characteristic polynomial is
 the minimal polynomial of a nonintegral Pisot number $\beta$, and fix
 $D\ge1$.  If $m\ge m_0(U,D)$, coefficients
 $e_t,\ldots,e_{t+m}\in[-D,D]\cap\mathbb Z$, and integers $k_t,k_{t+1}$
 satisfy
 \[
 \sum_{j=0}^{m-1}u_je_{t+j}=k_tu_m,
 \qquad
 \sum_{j=0}^{m-1}u_je_{t+1+j}=k_{t+1}u_m,
 \]
 then
 \[
 k_{t+1}=0,\qquad e_{t+m}=-k_t.
 \]
 \end{lemma}
 
 \begin{proof}
 The growth bounds give, for either row,
 \[
 |k_t|\le D\frac{\sum_{j<m}u_j}{u_m}
  <\frac{DA_U}{b_U(\beta-1)}\le K_{U,D},
 \]
 and similarly $|k_{t+1}|\le K_{U,D}$.  For every embedding set
 \[
 A_{t,i}=\sum_{j=0}^{m-1}e_{t+j}\beta_i^j-k_t\beta_i^m.
 \]
 The recurrence and its Binet expansion give $\sum_i c_iA_{t,i}=0$.
 For $i\ge1$,
 \[
 |A_{t,i}|\le\frac{D}{1-|\beta_i|}+K_{U,D},
 \]
 so $|A_{t,0}|\le M_{U,D}$, and the same holds at $t+1$.
 Direct subtraction of the adjacent dominant-embedding sums gives the exact
 identity
 \begin{equation}
 \beta^m(e_{t+m}+k_t-\beta k_{t+1})
 =\beta A_{t+1,0}-A_{t,0}+e_t.
 \label{eq:bounded-zero-adjacent-identity}
 \end{equation}
 The right side has modulus at most $B_{U,D}$.  If the parenthesis on the
 left were nonzero, \eqref{eq:bounded-zero-separation} and
 \eqref{eq:bounded-zero-contraction-threshold} would make the left side
 strictly larger than $B_{U,D}$, a contradiction.  Hence
 $e_{t+m}+k_t=\beta k_{t+1}$.  Irrationality of $\beta$ and integrality of
 the three coefficients force $k_{t+1}=0$ and $e_{t+m}=-k_t$.
 \end{proof}
 
 \begin{theorem}[Linear transient bound for bounded $U$-representations of zero]
 \label{thm:bounded-zero-linear-transient}
 Let $U=(u_n)_{n\ge0}$ be a strictly increasing sequence of positive integers
 with $u_0=1$, satisfying the integral recurrence whose characteristic
 polynomial is the minimal polynomial of a nonintegral Pisot number $\beta$.
-Fix $D\ge1$.  For $m\ge2$, let $G_m(U,D)$ be the directed graph with vertex
-set
-\[
-([-D,D]\cap\mathbb Z)^{m-1}.
-\]
-There is an edge
-\[
-(e_0,\ldots,e_{m-2})\longrightarrow
-(e_1,\ldots,e_{m-2},e_{m-1})
-\]
-if and only if there is an integer $c$ such that
-\begin{equation}
-e_0u_0+e_1u_1+\cdots+e_{m-1}u_{m-1}+cu_m=0.
-\label{eq:bounded-zero-overlap-edge-theorem}
-\end{equation}
-The completing coefficient is unique and necessarily satisfies
+Fix $D\ge1$, and let $G_m(U,D)$ be the graph defined above.  For every edge,
+the completing coefficient is unique and necessarily satisfies
 $|c|\le K_{U,D}$.
 Then there is an effectively computable $C(U,D)<\infty$ such that, for every
 $m\ge2$, if no directed cycle of $G_m(U,D)$ is reachable from a vertex whose
 first coordinate is nonzero, every directed path beginning at such a vertex
 has fewer than $C(U,D)m$ edges.
 
 Equivalently, after shifting a chain so that its initial exposed coefficient
 is nonzero, either it reaches a directed cycle, or it has fewer than
 $C(U,D)m$ further overlaps.  The graphs are finite-state recognizers of these
 overlap chains, and their edges are bounded $U$-representations of zero by
-\eqref{eq:bounded-zero-overlap-edge-theorem}.
+\eqref{eq:bounded-zero-overlap-edge}.
 \end{theorem}
 
 \begin{proof}
 For an edge at time $t$, write $k_t=-c$ in
-\eqref{eq:bounded-zero-overlap-edge-theorem}.  The growth estimate gives
+\eqref{eq:bounded-zero-overlap-edge}.  The growth estimate gives
 $|k_t|\le K_{U,D}$ uniformly in $m$.  Suppose first that
 $m\ge m_0(U,D)$.  Along any path of at least two edges, Lemma
 \ref{lem:bounded-zero-arbitrary-alphabet-collapse}, applied to each adjacent
 pair, gives
 \[
 k_1=k_2=\cdots=0,\qquad
 e_m=-k_0,\qquad e_{m+1}=e_{m+2}=\cdots=0.
 \]
 A path of $m+1$ edges therefore reaches the all-zero vertex.  That vertex
 has a loop, so such a path would make a directed cycle reachable from its
 initial vertex.  Under the hypothesis of the theorem, every path has at
 most $m$ edges for $m\ge m_0(U,D)$.
 
 For each of the finitely many $2\le m<m_0(U,D)$, the graph is finite.  When
 no cycle is reachable from the first-coordinate-nonzero vertices, exact
 acyclic longest-path search computes the maximum $p_{U,D}(m)$; set it to
 zero otherwise.  Thus, for example,
 \[
 C(U,D)=\max\left(2,
  \max_{2\le m<m_0(U,D)}
  \left\lceil\frac{1+p_{U,D}(m)}m\right\rceil\right)
 \]
 is effective and has the required property, with the inner maximum understood
 as zero when $m_0(U,D)=2$.
 \end{proof}
 
-The theorem also gives the useful global chain dichotomy.  Consider all
-bounded zero words of length $m+1$ that overlap one digit at a time.  Either
-some chain with a nonzero coefficient has a finite prefix followed by a
-periodic bounded zero representation, or every nonzero chain has fewer than

-the absence of such a cycle, at most $m-1$ initial shifts pass before a
-nonzero coordinate occupies the first position, after which the theorem
+\begin{corollary}[Global chain dichotomy]
+\label{cor:bounded-zero-global-dichotomy}
+Under the hypotheses of Theorem~\ref{thm:bounded-zero-linear-transient}, fix
+$m\ge2$ and consider all bounded zero words of length $m+1$ that overlap one
+digit at a time.  Either some chain with a nonzero coefficient has a finite
+prefix followed by a periodic bounded zero representation, or every nonzero
+chain has fewer than $(C(U,D)+1)m$ rows.
+\end{corollary}
+
+\begin{proof}
+A reachable cycle gives the periodic tail.  In the absence of such a cycle,
+at most $m-1$ initial shifts pass before a nonzero coordinate occupies the
+first position, after which Theorem~\ref{thm:bounded-zero-linear-transient}
 bounds the remaining overlaps.  The alternatives are mutually exclusive
 because a periodic tail can be repeated to make arbitrarily long chains.
+\end{proof}
 
-\begin{remark}[Logical boundary of the standard-object theorem]
+\begin{remark}[Arithmetic scope]
 \label{rem:bounded-zero-standard-object-boundary}
-The statement and proof of Theorem~\ref{thm:bounded-zero-linear-transient}
-use only a fixed Pisot recurrence, bounded integer coefficients, the
-distinguished weight $u_m$, and one-position overlaps of bounded
-$U$-representations of zero.  They use no canonical rank bijection,
-legal-word alphabet, cyclic reduction map, positive/negative realization of
-coefficients, image shift, or inverse decoder.  The distinguished modulus
-$u_m$ and consecutive recurrence weights remain essential: replacing $u_m$
-by an unrelated modulus destroys \eqref{eq:bounded-zero-adjacent-identity}.
-The theorem therefore does not concern numerical $\beta$-normalization,
-successor carry propagation, general synchronized transductions, general
-right-closing maps, or a broad class of sliding block codes.
+Theorem~\ref{thm:bounded-zero-linear-transient} is independent of the cyclic
+rank construction of Section~\ref{sec:cyclic-rank-application}.  Its
+essential arithmetic features are the distinguished terminal weight $u_m$
+and the use of consecutive recurrence weights; replacing $u_m$ by an
+unrelated modulus removes the identity underlying Lemma
+\ref{lem:bounded-zero-arbitrary-alphabet-collapse}.  These features are what
+make the linear estimate available for the overlap graphs considered here.
 \end{remark}

 \section{A fixed cubic system with exact linear depth}
 \label{sec:fixed-cubic-sharpness}
 
 Let $\theta$ be the real root of $x^3-2x^2+x-1$ and define
 $U_\theta=(Q_j(\theta))_{j\ge0}$ by
 \begin{equation}
 Q_0(\theta)=1,\quad Q_1(\theta)=2,\quad Q_2(\theta)=4,
 \quad Q_{j+3}(\theta)=2Q_{j+2}(\theta)-Q_{j+1}(\theta)+Q_j(\theta).
 \label{eq:theta-count-recurrence}
 \end{equation}
 The canonical digit and difference alphabets will be $\{0,1\}$ and
 $\{-1,0,1\}$, respectively.  For $m\ge4$ put
 \[
 \lambda_m=2\left\lfloor\frac m2\right\rfloor-1
 \]
 and define the signed terminal words by
 \begin{equation}
 \begin{gathered}
 E_4=(1,-1,-1,-1,1),\\
 E_m=(1,0,-E_{m-2},0,0)\quad(m\ge6\text{ even}),\\
 E_m=(E_{m-1},0)\quad(m\ge5\text{ odd}).
 \end{gathered}
 \label{eq:theta-terminal-word}
 \end{equation}
 Here juxtaposition denotes concatenation and a prefixed minus changes every
 sign.
 
 \begin{theorem}[Fixed cubic sharpness and terminal classification]
 \label{thm:fixed-cubic-sharpness}
 For every $m\ge4$,
 \begin{equation*}
 \Phi_{\theta,m}\text{ is injective},\qquad
 \ell_{\rm cau}(\theta,m)=2\left\lfloor\frac m2\right\rfloor-1.
 \tag{A2}\label{eq:pisot-linear-A2}
 \end{equation*}
 More precisely,
 \begin{equation*}
 \mathcal N_{\theta,m,\lambda_m-1}=\{E_m,-E_m\}\ne\varnothing,
 \qquad
 \mathcal N_{\theta,m,\lambda_m}=\varnothing.
 \tag{A3}\label{eq:pisot-linear-A3}
 \end{equation*}
 Consequently
 \[
 \lim_{m\to\infty}\frac{\ell_{\rm cau}(\theta,m)}m=1.
 \]
 Moreover, in the bounded-zero graph $G_m(U_\theta,1)$ the maximum path
 length from a first-coordinate-nonzero vertex is $\lambda_m-1$, hence at
 least $m-3$.  Thus no uniform theorem for the fixed systems covered by
 \textup{(A1)}, and no uniform analogue of
 Theorem~\ref{thm:bounded-zero-linear-transient}, can replace the linear order
-by $o(m)$.  This does not assert that the effective constants in those
-results are numerically optimal or that coefficient one is universal across
-systems.
+by $o(m)$.
 \end{theorem}

+The identity $d_\theta(1)=1101\,0^\infty$ supplies the legal language and its

+for $Q_j(\theta)$, a direct quotient bound, the contracting-conjugate
+estimate, the suffix-sum identity, and the terminal integer equations.

+Sharpness here concerns growth order for the fixed recurrence $U_\theta$ and
+the fixed coefficient alphabet $\{-1,0,1\}$.  The effective constants in
+Theorem~\ref{thm:bounded-zero-linear-transient} and Corollary
+\ref{cor:pisot-linear-causal-depth} are not claimed to be numerically
+optimal across systems.
+\end{remark}

-Thus $\theta$ is Pisot.  Its defining equation gives
+Writing $f(x)=x^3-2x^2+x-1$, we have
+$f(7/4)=-1/64$ and $f(351/200)=3151/8000000$.  The critical points of $f$
+are $1/3$ and $1$, and $f$ is negative at both, so these endpoint values
+isolate its only real zero.  The product of the two nonreal roots has modulus
+$1/\theta$, and hence $\theta$ is Pisot.  Its defining equation gives
 \[
 1=\theta^{-1}+\theta^{-2}+\theta^{-4},
 \]
 and every proper suffix of $1101\,0^\infty$ is lexicographically smaller.
 Parry's criterion therefore gives $d_\theta(1)=1101\,0^\infty$, and the
 canonical language counts are exactly \eqref{eq:theta-count-recurrence}.
 In particular, the digit and difference alphabets are $\{0,1\}$ and
 $\{-1,0,1\}$.
 
 The recurrence also implies the positive recurrence
 $Q_{n+4}=Q_{n+3}+Q_{n+2}+Q_n$.  Thus
 $2Q_{n+1}-3Q_n>0$ follows from its four positive initial values.  Starting
 with $Q_0<2Q_1$ and using
 $\sum_{j<m+1}Q_j<3Q_m<2Q_{m+1}$ then gives
 \begin{equation}
 \sum_{j<m}Q_j(\theta)<2Q_m(\theta).
 \label{eq:theta-carry-one}
 \end{equation}
 Every quotient in \eqref{eq:pisot-window-carry} therefore belongs to
 $\{-1,0,1\}$.
 
 We verify the contraction threshold explicitly.  If
 $\rho,\overline\rho$ are the nonreal roots, write
 \[
 Q_n=c_\theta\theta^n+c_\rho\rho^n+
 \overline{c_\rho}\,\overline\rho^n.
 \]
 The rational isolating interval above and the residue formula for
 \[
 \sum_{n\ge0}Q_nz^n=\frac{1+z^2}{1-2z+z^2-z^3}
 \]
 give
 \[
 1.26<c_\theta<1.27,\qquad |c_\rho|<0.19,
 \qquad |\rho|<0.756.
 \]
 Here $c_\alpha=(\alpha^2+1)/(3\alpha^2-4\alpha+1)$ for every root
 $\alpha$, and $|\rho|=\theta^{-1/2}$.  All displayed decimals are terminating
 rational bounds obtained by substitution of the endpoints $7/4$ and
 $351/200$ and exact rational arithmetic.  The dominant-embedding error in
 the proof of Lemma~\ref{lem:bounded-zero-arbitrary-alphabet-collapse} is
 therefore less than $1.54$.  For the actual quotient set
 $\{-1,0,1\}$, the nonzero separation in
 $e_{t+m}+k_t-\theta k_{t+1}$ is at least

 \[

 >1+(1+\theta)1.54.
 \]
 Repeating the exact identity
 \eqref{eq:bounded-zero-adjacent-identity} with this sharpened finite quotient
 set gives
 \begin{equation}
 k_{t+1}=0,\qquad e_{t+m}=-k_t
 \label{eq:theta-carry-collapse}
 \end{equation}
 for every adjacent pair of collision rows and every $m\ge6$.  The apertures

 least two collision rows.  After \eqref{eq:theta-carry-collapse}, all its
 coordinates beyond $m$ vanish except possibly $e_m=-k_0$.  Define the full

 \[

 \]
 Every collision row with $t\ge1$ is now $R_t=0$, and the first row together
 with $e_m=-k_0$ gives $R_0=0$.  The recurrence implies the exact identity
 \begin{equation}
 R_t=e_t+e_{t+2}+2R_{t+1}-R_{t+2}+R_{t+3}.
 \label{eq:theta-suffix-renormalization}
 \end{equation}
 Thus four consecutive zero suffix sums force $e_t=-e_{t+2}$.

 $R_t=0$ for $0\le t\le m-3$.  The terminal equation is
 \[
 e_{m-3}+2e_{m-2}+4e_{m-1}+7e_m=0.
 \]

 \section{Cyclic rank recodings and the collision quotient}
 \label{sec:cyclic-rank-application}
 
+We now code the full shift on the canonical digit alphabet by replacing each
+raw length-$m$ window with the legal word of the same cyclic rank.  A
+future-only inverse recovers the raw symbol at time zero from output windows
+beginning at time zero, without using past output.  Equality of two folded
+windows says precisely that their raw ranks are congruent modulo $u_m$.
+Taking coordinatewise differences converts a run of such congruences into
+overlapping bounded representations of zero.  The exactness of this quotient
+is crucial: it identifies all collisions, so path length in the arithmetic
+graph measures inverse depth rather than merely bounding it.
+
 A Pisot linear numeration system is a sequence $U=(u_j)_{j\ge0}$ of positive
 integers satisfying the recurrence whose characteristic polynomial is the
-minimal polynomial of a Pisot number.  Its initial values need not be the
-standard ones.  Here ``standard'' has its technical meaning
-$u_{-1}=\cdots=u_{-d+1}=0$, $u_0=1$; we do not use it as a synonym for the
-larger class considered below.  Assume that $U$ is strictly increasing and
-$u_0=1$, and let
+minimal polynomial of a Pisot number.  We allow nonstandard initial values
+and assume that $U$ is strictly increasing with $u_0=1$.  Let
 \[
 D_U=\{0,\ldots,d_U\}
 \]
 be its canonical greedy digit set.  The canonical greedy $U$-normal words of
 length $m$ are
 \begin{equation}
 X_{U,m}=\left\{x\in D_U^m:
  \sum_{j=0}^k x_ju_j<u_{k+1}\quad(0\le k<m)\right\}.
 \label{eq:pisot-canonical-words}
 \end{equation}
 Consequently
 \[
 \Val_{U,m}(x)=\sum_{j=0}^{m-1}x_ju_j
 \]
 maps $X_{U,m}$ bijectively onto $\{0,\ldots,u_m-1\}$.  Define
 \begin{equation}
 \Fold_{U,m}(w)=
 (\Val_{U,m}|_{X_{U,m}})^{-1}
        (\Val_{U,m}(w)\bmod u_m)
 \label{eq:pisot-canonical-fold}
 \end{equation}
 and let $\Phi_{U,m}$ be the corresponding sliding code on
 $D_U^{\mathbb Z}$.  This is the cyclic language-rank recoding: each output
 word is the unique legal word having the cyclically reduced rank of the raw
 window.
 
 Define $\ell_{\rm cau}(U,m)$ to be the least $L\ge1$ for which the raw digit
 at time zero is determined, on the image of $\Phi_{U,m}$, by the output
 windows at times $0,\ldots,L-1$; set it to $\infty$ if no such $L$ exists.
 Thus $\ell_{\rm cau}(U,m)-1$ is the least anticipation of an inverse
 constrained to have memory zero.  Relabeling each output by its canonical
 rank turns the local rule into
 \[
 (x_0,\ldots,x_{m-1})\longmapsto
 \sum_{j=0}^{m-1}u_jx_j\pmod {u_m}.
 \]
-This symbolwise relabeling is bijective.  The rule is not a linear cellular
-automaton: in general $D_U$ is only a digit subset of
-$\mathbb Z/u_m\mathbb Z$, not a module on which the rule is a group
-endomorphism.
+This symbolwise relabeling is bijective.
 
 Put $\Delta_U=[-d_U,d_U]\cap\mathbb Z$ and, for $L\ge1$, set
 \begin{equation}
 \mathcal N_{U,m,L}=\left\{e\in\Delta_U^{m+L-1}:
  e_0\ne0,\quad
  \sum_{j=0}^{m-1}u_je_{t+j}\equiv0\pmod {u_m}
  \ (0\le t<L)\right\}.
 \label{eq:pisot-bad-sliding-set}
 \end{equation}
 Equivalently, $\mathcal N_{U,m,L}$ is the finite-block kernel of the
 $L$-row banded Toeplitz operator with kernel
 $(u_0,\ldots,u_{m-1})$ over $\mathbb Z/u_m\mathbb Z$, restricted by
 $e_0\ne0$ and by the coefficient bound $\Delta_U$.  Every row has a unique
 integer quotient $k_t$:
 \begin{equation}
 \sum_{j=0}^{m-1}u_je_{t+j}=k_tu_m.
 \label{eq:pisot-window-carry}
 \end{equation}
 Equivalently,
 \begin{equation}
 [e_t,e_{t+1},\ldots,e_{t+m-1},-k_t]_U=0,
 \label{eq:pisot-zero-word-bridge}
 \end{equation}
 where the last coordinate has weight $u_m$.  Thus every collision row is an
 edge of $G_m(U,d_U)$, with completing coefficient $-k_t$.
 
 \begin{lemma}[Exact collision quotient for $U$]
 \label{lem:pisot-exact-collision-quotient}
 For every $m\ge2$,
 \begin{equation}
 \ell_{\rm cau}(U,m)
 =\min\{L\ge1:\mathcal N_{U,m,L}=\varnothing\}.
 \label{eq:pisot-exact-depth}
 \end{equation}
 Let $\Gamma_{U,m}$ have vertex set $\Delta_U^{m-1}$ and an edge
 $(v_0,\ldots,v_{m-2})\to(v_1,\ldots,v_{m-2},a)$ exactly when
 \[
 \sum_{j=0}^{m-2}u_jv_j+u_{m-1}a\equiv0\pmod {u_m}.
 \]
 If $B_{U,m}$ is the set of vertices with first coordinate nonzero, then
 \begin{equation}
 \ell_{\rm cau}(U,m)=1+\sup\{\text{edge length of a path from }B_{U,m}\}.
 \label{eq:pisot-longest-path}
 \end{equation}
 Moreover, $\Phi_{U,m}$ is injective if and only if no directed cycle of
 $\Gamma_{U,m}$ is reachable from $B_{U,m}$.
 \end{lemma}
 
 \begin{proof}
 Two folded windows agree exactly when the difference of their raw values is
 divisible by $u_m$, because the canonical normal words represent
 $0,\ldots,u_m-1$ bijectively.  Every $e\in\Delta_U$ is realized as a
 difference of two digits by taking its positive and negative parts in
 $D_U$.  Hence two output $L$-blocks with distinct initial raw digits are
 equivalent to a vector in $\mathcal N_{U,m,L}$, with no lost collisions.
 Reading consecutive difference windows gives precisely the paths from
 $B_{U,m}$ in $\Gamma_{U,m}$, proving \eqref{eq:pisot-exact-depth} and
 \eqref{eq:pisot-longest-path}.  A reachable nonzero cycle can be repeated
 periodically and is realized by two raw points.
 
 It remains only to note why injectivity rules out the zero loop as a
 reachable cycle.  If $d_U\ge u_m$, the constant nonzero difference sequence
 $e_t=u_m$ belongs to $\Delta_U^{\mathbb Z}$ and every window sum is divisible
 by $u_m$; hence $\Phi_{U,m}$ is noninjective and the difference graph has a
 nonzero loop.  In the only remaining case, $d_U<u_m$.  If a vertex has an
 edge to the zero vertex, its overlap coordinates vanish and its first
 coordinate $a$ satisfies $a\equiv0\pmod {u_m}$.  The bound
 $|a|\le d_U<u_m$ gives $a=0$, so the zero vertex is its own unique
 predecessor.  This proves the last assertion without imposing a restriction
 on the allowed nonstandard initial values.
 \end{proof}
 
 \begin{corollary}[Aperture-linear future-only inverse depth]
 \label{cor:pisot-linear-causal-depth}
 Let $U$ be any strictly increasing Pisot numeration system with $u_0=1$,
 canonical greedy digit set and canonical normal words.  There is an
 effectively computable $C_U<\infty$ such that, for every $m\ge2$,
 \begin{equation*}
 \Phi_{U,m}\text{ injective}
 \quad\Longrightarrow\quad
 \ell_{\rm cau}(U,m)\le C_Um.
 \tag{A1}\label{eq:pisot-linear-A1}
 \end{equation*}
 Equivalently, if no first-coordinate-nonzero state of $\Gamma_{U,m}$ can
 reach a directed cycle, every directed path from that initial state set has
 length at most $C_Um-1$.
 \end{corollary}
 
 \begin{proof}
 If the Pisot root has degree one, then $u_j=b^j$,
 $D_U=\{0,\ldots,b-1\}$, the fold is the identity, and
 $\ell_{\rm cau}(U,m)=1$; take $C_U=1$.  Otherwise apply
 Theorem~\ref{thm:bounded-zero-linear-transient} with $D=d_U$.  Its graph is
 exactly $\Gamma_{U,m}$, because \eqref{eq:pisot-zero-word-bridge} is the
-edge condition \eqref{eq:bounded-zero-overlap-edge-theorem}.  By Lemma
+edge condition \eqref{eq:bounded-zero-overlap-edge}.  By Lemma
 \ref{lem:pisot-exact-collision-quotient}, injectivity is equivalent to the
 absence of a reachable cycle and the causal length is one plus the longest
 path length.  Since the theorem gives fewer than $C(U,d_U)m$ edges, the
 integer path length is at most $C(U,d_U)m-1$.  Taking
 $C_U=C(U,d_U)$ proves both formulations.
 \end{proof}
 
-The scope is not a disguised standard-initial-value convention.  For
-example, standard $k$-bonacci initial conditions begin $1,1,\ldots$ and fail
-strict increase, whereas the customary shift beginning $1,2,\ldots$ belongs
-to the present class.  Nor is Condition~F assumed: the principal root of
-$x^3-3x^2+2x-1$ is Pisot but does not satisfy Condition~F
-\cite[Theorem~3]{Akiyama2000}.  The equivalence of Condition~F with zero
-preservation is proved in \cite{CartonSudberyYassawi2026}, but neither
-property enters the corollary.
+The corollary applies to nonstandard initial values whenever the resulting
+sequence is strictly increasing; for example, the customary shifted
+$k$-bonacci sequence beginning $1,2,\ldots$ belongs to this class.  It also
+applies beyond Condition~F: the principal root of $x^3-3x^2+2x-1$ is Pisot
+but does not satisfy Condition~F \cite[Theorem~3]{Akiyama2000}.  The proof
+uses only the Pisot embeddings and the recurrence weights.

 \section{One-position overlap chains of bounded zero representations}
 \label{sec:bounded-zero-transients}
 
 Let $U=(u_n)_{n\ge0}$ be a strictly increasing sequence of positive
 integers with $u_0=1$.  We assume that $U$ satisfies the integral recurrence
 whose characteristic polynomial is the minimal polynomial of a nonintegral
 Pisot number $\beta$.  Thus, if
 $\beta=\beta_0,\beta_1,\ldots,\beta_{s-1}$ are the roots of that polynomial,
 then $\beta>1$ and $|\beta_i|<1$ for $i\ge1$.  The recurrence and the initial
 values effectively give algebraic numbers $c_i$ such that
 \begin{equation}
 u_n=\sum_{i=0}^{s-1}c_i\beta_i^n,
 \qquad c_0>0.
 \label{eq:pisot-binet}
 \end{equation}
 Effective root isolation gives positive algebraic bounds $b_U,A_U$ with
 \begin{equation}
 b_U\beta^n\le u_n\le A_U\beta^n\qquad(n\ge0).
 \label{eq:pisot-effective-growth}
 \end{equation}
 Indeed, one isolates an index after which the total conjugate contribution
 in \eqref{eq:pisot-binet} is at most $c_0\beta^n/2$ and inspects the
 remaining finite prefix.
 
 Fix $D\ge1$.  For $m\ge2$, let $G_m(U,D)$ be the directed graph with vertex
 set
 \[
 ([-D,D]\cap\mathbb Z)^{m-1}.
 \]
 There is an edge
 \[
 (e_0,\ldots,e_{m-2})\longrightarrow
 (e_1,\ldots,e_{m-2},e_{m-1})
 \]
 if and only if there is an integer $c$ such that
 \begin{equation}
 e_0u_0+e_1u_1+\cdots+e_{m-1}u_{m-1}+cu_m=0.
 \label{eq:bounded-zero-overlap-edge}
 \end{equation}
 An edge is therefore a bounded $U$-representation of zero, organized so
 that consecutive representations overlap in one position.  A path is a
 one-position overlap chain.  We distinguish the initial vertices whose
 first coordinate is nonzero because an arbitrary nonzero chain can be
 shifted until its first exposed nonzero coefficient occupies that position.
 
 Put
 \begin{equation}
 K_{U,D}=\left\lceil\frac{DA_U}{b_U(\beta-1)}\right\rceil.
 \label{eq:bounded-zero-carry-bound}
 \end{equation}
 The coefficient $c$ completing an edge is unique and satisfies
 $|c|\le K_{U,D}$: uniqueness follows from $u_m>0$, and
 \[
 |c|\le D\frac{\sum_{j<m}u_j}{u_m}
  <\frac{DA_U}{b_U(\beta-1)}.
 \]
+The proof compares adjacent edges through all embeddings of the Pisot field.
+The following constants bound the conjugate contributions and separate the
+finitely many possible dominant-embedding defects from zero.
 Define the effective positive separation
 \begin{equation}
 \delta_{U,D}=\min\left\{|a+k-\beta l|:
  |a|\le D,\ |k|,|l|\le K_{U,D},\ a+k-\beta l\ne0\right\}.
 \label{eq:bounded-zero-separation}
 \end{equation}
 The variables in this minimum are integers.  Since $\beta$ is irrational,
 $\delta_{U,D}>0$, and exact algebraic-number comparison computes it.  Set
 \begin{align}
 M_{U,D}&=\frac1{c_0}\sum_{i=1}^{s-1}|c_i|
  \left(\frac{D}{1-|\beta_i|}+K_{U,D}\right),\nonumber\\
 B_{U,D}&=D+(1+\beta)M_{U,D},
 \label{eq:bounded-zero-conjugate-bound}
 \end{align}
 and
 \begin{equation}
 m_0(U,D)=\min\{m\ge2:\beta^m\delta_{U,D}>B_{U,D}\}.
 \label{eq:bounded-zero-contraction-threshold}
 \end{equation}
 All these constants are effective from $U$ and $D$.
 
 \begin{lemma}[Adjacent collapse for an arbitrary bounded coefficient alphabet]
 \label{lem:bounded-zero-arbitrary-alphabet-collapse}
 Let $U$ satisfy the integral recurrence whose characteristic polynomial is
 the minimal polynomial of a nonintegral Pisot number $\beta$, and fix
 $D\ge1$.  If $m\ge m_0(U,D)$, coefficients
 $e_t,\ldots,e_{t+m}\in[-D,D]\cap\mathbb Z$, and integers $k_t,k_{t+1}$
 satisfy
 \[
 \sum_{j=0}^{m-1}u_je_{t+j}=k_tu_m,
 \qquad
 \sum_{j=0}^{m-1}u_je_{t+1+j}=k_{t+1}u_m,
 \]
 then
 \[
 k_{t+1}=0,\qquad e_{t+m}=-k_t.
 \]
 \end{lemma}
 
 \begin{proof}
 The growth bounds give, for either row,
 \[
 |k_t|\le D\frac{\sum_{j<m}u_j}{u_m}
  <\frac{DA_U}{b_U(\beta-1)}\le K_{U,D},
 \]
 and similarly $|k_{t+1}|\le K_{U,D}$.  For every embedding set
 \[
 A_{t,i}=\sum_{j=0}^{m-1}e_{t+j}\beta_i^j-k_t\beta_i^m.
 \]
 The recurrence and its Binet expansion give $\sum_i c_iA_{t,i}=0$.
 For $i\ge1$,
 \[
 |A_{t,i}|\le\frac{D}{1-|\beta_i|}+K_{U,D},
 \]
 so $|A_{t,0}|\le M_{U,D}$, and the same holds at $t+1$.
 Direct subtraction of the adjacent dominant-embedding sums gives the exact
 identity
 \begin{equation}
 \beta^m(e_{t+m}+k_t-\beta k_{t+1})
 =\beta A_{t+1,0}-A_{t,0}+e_t.
 \label{eq:bounded-zero-adjacent-identity}
 \end{equation}
 The right side has modulus at most $B_{U,D}$.  If the parenthesis on the
 left were nonzero, \eqref{eq:bounded-zero-separation} and
 \eqref{eq:bounded-zero-contraction-threshold} would make the left side
 strictly larger than $B_{U,D}$, a contradiction.  Hence
 $e_{t+m}+k_t=\beta k_{t+1}$.  Irrationality of $\beta$ and integrality of
 the three coefficients force $k_{t+1}=0$ and $e_{t+m}=-k_t$.
 \end{proof}
 
 \begin{theorem}[Linear transient bound for bounded $U$-representations of zero]
 \label{thm:bounded-zero-linear-transient}
 Let $U=(u_n)_{n\ge0}$ be a strictly increasing sequence of positive integers
 with $u_0=1$, satisfying the integral recurrence whose characteristic
 polynomial is the minimal polynomial of a nonintegral Pisot number $\beta$.
-Fix $D\ge1$.  For $m\ge2$, let $G_m(U,D)$ be the directed graph with vertex
-set
-\[
-([-D,D]\cap\mathbb Z)^{m-1}.
-\]
-There is an edge
-\[
-(e_0,\ldots,e_{m-2})\longrightarrow
-(e_1,\ldots,e_{m-2},e_{m-1})
-\]
-if and only if there is an integer $c$ such that
-\begin{equation}
-e_0u_0+e_1u_1+\cdots+e_{m-1}u_{m-1}+cu_m=0.
-\label{eq:bounded-zero-overlap-edge-theorem}
-\end{equation}
-The completing coefficient is unique and necessarily satisfies
+Fix $D\ge1$, and let $G_m(U,D)$ be the graph defined above.  For every edge,
+the completing coefficient is unique and necessarily satisfies
 $|c|\le K_{U,D}$.
 Then there is an effectively computable $C(U,D)<\infty$ such that, for every
 $m\ge2$, if no directed cycle of $G_m(U,D)$ is reachable from a vertex whose
 first coordinate is nonzero, every directed path beginning at such a vertex
 has fewer than $C(U,D)m$ edges.
 
 Equivalently, after shifting a chain so that its initial exposed coefficient
 is nonzero, either it reaches a directed cycle, or it has fewer than
 $C(U,D)m$ further overlaps.  The graphs are finite-state recognizers of these
 overlap chains, and their edges are bounded $U$-representations of zero by
-\eqref{eq:bounded-zero-overlap-edge-theorem}.
+\eqref{eq:bounded-zero-overlap-edge}.
 \end{theorem}
 
 \begin{proof}
 For an edge at time $t$, write $k_t=-c$ in
-\eqref{eq:bounded-zero-overlap-edge-theorem}.  The growth estimate gives
+\eqref{eq:bounded-zero-overlap-edge}.  The growth estimate gives
 $|k_t|\le K_{U,D}$ uniformly in $m$.  Suppose first that
 $m\ge m_0(U,D)$.  Along any path of at least two edges, Lemma
 \ref{lem:bounded-zero-arbitrary-alphabet-collapse}, applied to each adjacent
 pair, gives
 \[
 k_1=k_2=\cdots=0,\qquad
 e_m=-k_0,\qquad e_{m+1}=e_{m+2}=\cdots=0.
 \]
 A path of $m+1$ edges therefore reaches the all-zero vertex.  That vertex
 has a loop, so such a path would make a directed cycle reachable from its
 initial vertex.  Under the hypothesis of the theorem, every path has at
 most $m$ edges for $m\ge m_0(U,D)$.
 
 For each of the finitely many $2\le m<m_0(U,D)$, the graph is finite.  When
 no cycle is reachable from the first-coordinate-nonzero vertices, exact
 acyclic longest-path search computes the maximum $p_{U,D}(m)$; set it to
 zero otherwise.  Thus, for example,
 \[
 C(U,D)=\max\left(2,
  \max_{2\le m<m_0(U,D)}
  \left\lceil\frac{1+p_{U,D}(m)}m\right\rceil\right)
 \]
 is effective and has the required property, with the inner maximum understood
 as zero when $m_0(U,D)=2$.
 \end{proof}
 
-The theorem also gives the useful global chain dichotomy.  Consider all
-bounded zero words of length $m+1$ that overlap one digit at a time.  Either
-some chain with a nonzero coefficient has a finite prefix followed by a
-periodic bounded zero representation, or every nonzero chain has fewer than
-$(C(U,D)+1)m$ rows.  Indeed, a reachable cycle gives the periodic tail.  In
-the absence of such a cycle, at most $m-1$ initial shifts pass before a
-nonzero coordinate occupies the first position, after which the theorem
+\begin{corollary}[Global chain dichotomy]
+\label{cor:bounded-zero-global-dichotomy}
+Under the hypotheses of Theorem~\ref{thm:bounded-zero-linear-transient}, fix
+$m\ge2$ and consider all bounded zero words of length $m+1$ that overlap one
+digit at a time.  Either some chain with a nonzero coefficient has a finite
+prefix followed by a periodic bounded zero representation, or every nonzero
+chain has fewer than $(C(U,D)+1)m$ rows.
+\end{corollary}
+
+\begin{proof}
+A reachable cycle gives the periodic tail.  In the absence of such a cycle,
+at most $m-1$ initial shifts pass before a nonzero coordinate occupies the
+first position, after which Theorem~\ref{thm:bounded-zero-linear-transient}
 bounds the remaining overlaps.  The alternatives are mutually exclusive
 because a periodic tail can be repeated to make arbitrarily long chains.
+\end{proof}
 
-\begin{remark}[Logical boundary of the standard-object theorem]
+\begin{remark}[Arithmetic scope]
 \label{rem:bounded-zero-standard-object-boundary}
-The statement and proof of Theorem~\ref{thm:bounded-zero-linear-transient}
-use only a fixed Pisot recurrence, bounded integer coefficients, the
-distinguished weight $u_m$, and one-position overlaps of bounded
-$U$-representations of zero.  They use no canonical rank bijection,
-legal-word alphabet, cyclic reduction map, positive/negative realization of
-coefficients, image shift, or inverse decoder.  The distinguished modulus
-$u_m$ and consecutive recurrence weights remain essential: replacing $u_m$
-by an unrelated modulus destroys \eqref{eq:bounded-zero-adjacent-identity}.
-The theorem therefore does not concern numerical $\beta$-normalization,
-successor carry propagation, general synchronized transductions, general
-right-closing maps, or a broad class of sliding block codes.
+Theorem~\ref{thm:bounded-zero-linear-transient} is independent of the cyclic
+rank construction of Section~\ref{sec:cyclic-rank-application}.  Its
+essential arithmetic features are the distinguished terminal weight $u_m$
+and the use of consecutive recurrence weights; replacing $u_m$ by an
+unrelated modulus removes the identity underlying Lemma
+\ref{lem:bounded-zero-arbitrary-alphabet-collapse}.  These features are what
+make the linear estimate available for the overlap graphs considered here.
 \end{remark}

"C:\\Windows\\System32\\WindowsPowerShell\\v1.0\\powershell.exe" -Command '
$docs=@('"'main','cover_letter')
foreach("'$doc in $docs){
  $txt = (& pdftotext -layout "$doc.pdf" -) -join [Environment]::NewLine
  $info = & pdfinfo "$doc.pdf"
  $pages = ($info | Select-String '"'"'^Pages:'"\\s+(\\d+)' | ForEach-Object {"'$_.Matches[0].Groups[1].Value})
  $q = ([regex]::Matches($txt,[regex]::Escape('"'??'))).Count
  "'$controls = [regex]::Matches($txt,'"'\\\\[A-Za-z@]+') | ForEach-Object Value | Sort-Object -Unique
  "'$exts = [regex]::Matches($txt,'"'(?i)\\S*\\.(?:tex|pdf)\\S*') | ForEach-Object Value
  "'$strays = [regex]::Matches($txt,'"'(?i)(?<"'![A-Za-z])(qquad|quad|cdot|frac)(?![A-Za-z])'"') | ForEach-Object Value
  Write-Output \"DOC::"'$doc"
  Write-Output "PAGES::$pages"
  Write-Output "QUESTION_MARK_PAIRS::$q"
  Write-Output ("CONTROL_SEQUENCES::"+($(if($controls){$controls -join '"'|'}else{'NONE'})))
  Write-Output (\"TEX_PDF_OCCURRENCES::\"+("'$(if($exts){$exts -join '"'|'}else{'NONE'})))
  Write-Output (\"STRAY_TOKENS::\"+("'$(if($strays){$strays -join '"'|'}else{'NONE'})))
}
foreach("'$log in @('"'main.log','cover_letter.log')){
  "'$lines=Get-Content $log
  $undefRefs=@($lines | Select-String '"'LaTeX Warning: Reference .* undefined')
  "'$undefCites=@($lines | Select-String '"'LaTeX Warning: Citation .* undefined')
  "'$multi=@($lines | Select-String '"'(?i)(multiply defined|multiple definitions)')
  Write-Output \"LOG::"'$log"
  Write-Output "UNDEFINED_REFERENCES::$($undefRefs.Count)"
  Write-Output "UNDEFINED_CITATIONS::$($undefCites.Count)"
  Write-Output "MULTIPLY_DEFINED_LABELS::$($multi.Count)"
  if($undefRefs){$undefRefs.Line}
  if($undefCites){$undefCites.Line}
  if($multi){$multi.Line}
}' in D:\omega\automath\papers\publication\2026_linear_overlap_transients_bounded_zero_pisot_etds

OLD_THEOREM_2_2
\begin{theorem}[Linear transient bound for bounded $U$-representations of zero]
\label{thm:bounded-zero-linear-transient}
Let $U=(u_n)_{n\ge0}$ be a strictly increasing sequence of positive integers
with $u_0=1$, satisfying the integral recurrence whose characteristic
polynomial is the minimal polynomial of a nonintegral Pisot number $\beta$.
Fix $D\ge1$.  For $m\ge2$, let $G_m(U,D)$ be the directed graph with vertex
set
\[
([-D,D]\cap\mathbb Z)^{m-1}.
\]
There is an edge
\[
(e_0,\ldots,e_{m-2})\longrightarrow
(e_1,\ldots,e_{m-2},e_{m-1})
\]
if and only if there is an integer $c$ such that
\begin{equation}
e_0u_0+e_1u_1+\cdots+e_{m-1}u_{m-1}+cu_m=0.
\label{eq:bounded-zero-overlap-edge-theorem}
\end{equation}
The completing coefficient is unique and necessarily satisfies
$|c|\le K_{U,D}$.
Then there is an effectively computable $C(U,D)<\infty$ such that, for every
$m\ge2$, if no directed cycle of $G_m(U,D)$ is reachable from a vertex whose
first coordinate is nonzero, every directed path beginning at such a vertex
has fewer than $C(U,D)m$ edges.

Equivalently, after shifting a chain so that its initial exposed coefficient
is nonzero, either it reaches a directed cycle, or it has fewer than
$C(U,D)m$ further overlaps.  The graphs are finite-state recognizers of these
overlap chains, and their edges are bounded $U$-representations of zero by
\eqref{eq:bounded-zero-overlap-edge-theorem}.
\end{theorem}
NEW_THEOREM_2_2
\begin{theorem}[Linear transient bound for bounded $U$-representations of zero]
\label{thm:bounded-zero-linear-transient}
Let $U=(u_n)_{n\ge0}$ be a strictly increasing sequence of positive integers
with $u_0=1$, satisfying the integral recurrence whose characteristic
polynomial is the minimal polynomial of a nonintegral Pisot number $\beta$.
Fix $D\ge1$, and let $G_m(U,D)$ be the graph defined above.  For every edge,
the completing coefficient is unique and necessarily satisfies
$|c|\le K_{U,D}$.
Then there is an effectively computable $C(U,D)<\infty$ such that, for every
$m\ge2$, if no directed cycle of $G_m(U,D)$ is reachable from a vertex whose
first coordinate is nonzero, every directed path beginning at such a vertex
has fewer than $C(U,D)m$ edges.

Equivalently, after shifting a chain so that its initial exposed coefficient
is nonzero, either it reaches a directed cycle, or it has fewer than
$C(U,D)m$ further overlaps.  The graphs are finite-state recognizers of these
overlap chains, and their edges are bounded $U$-representations of zero by
\eqref{eq:bounded-zero-overlap-edge}.
\end{theorem}
OLD_COROLLARY_2_3_PROSE
The theorem also gives the useful global chain dichotomy.  Consider all
bounded zero words of length $m+1$ that overlap one digit at a time.  Either
some chain with a nonzero coefficient has a finite prefix followed by a
periodic bounded zero representation, or every nonzero chain has fewer than
$(C(U,D)+1)m$ rows.  Indeed, a reachable cycle gives the periodic tail.  In
the absence of such a cycle, at most $m-1$ initial shifts pass before a
nonzero coordinate occupies the first position, after which the theorem
bounds the remaining overlaps.  The alternatives are mutually exclusive
because a periodic tail can be repeated to make arbitrarily long chains.
NEW_COROLLARY_2_3
\begin{corollary}[Global chain dichotomy]
\label{cor:bounded-zero-global-dichotomy}
Under the hypotheses of Theorem~\ref{thm:bounded-zero-linear-transient}, fix
$m\ge2$ and consider all bounded zero words of length $m+1$ that overlap one
digit at a time.  Either some chain with a nonzero coefficient has a finite
prefix followed by a periodic bounded zero representation, or every nonzero
chain has fewer than $(C(U,D)+1)m$ rows.
\end{corollary}
OLD_REMARK_2_4
\begin{remark}[Logical boundary of the standard-object theorem]
\label{rem:bounded-zero-standard-object-boundary}
The statement and proof of Theorem~\ref{thm:bounded-zero-linear-transient}
use only a fixed Pisot recurrence, bounded integer coefficients, the
distinguished weight $u_m$, and one-position overlaps of bounded
$U$-representations of zero.  They use no canonical rank bijection,
legal-word alphabet, cyclic reduction map, positive/negative realization of
coefficients, image shift, or inverse decoder.  The distinguished modulus
$u_m$ and consecutive recurrence weights remain essential: replacing $u_m$
by an unrelated modulus destroys \eqref{eq:bounded-zero-adjacent-identity}.
The theorem therefore does not concern numerical $\beta$-normalization,
successor carry propagation, general synchronized transductions, general
right-closing maps, or a broad class of sliding block codes.
\end{remark}
NEW_REMARK_2_4
\begin{remark}[Arithmetic scope]
\label{rem:bounded-zero-standard-object-boundary}
Theorem~\ref{thm:bounded-zero-linear-transient} is independent of the cyclic
rank construction of Section~\ref{sec:cyclic-rank-application}.  Its
essential arithmetic features are the distinguished terminal weight $u_m$
and the use of consecutive recurrence weights; replacing $u_m$ by an
unrelated modulus removes the identity underlying Lemma
\ref{lem:bounded-zero-arbitrary-alphabet-collapse}.  These features are what
make the linear estimate available for the overlap graphs considered here.
\end{remark}
OLD_THEOREM_4_1
\begin{theorem}[Fixed cubic sharpness and terminal classification]
\label{thm:fixed-cubic-sharpness}
For every $m\ge4$,
\begin{equation*}
\Phi_{\theta,m}\text{ is injective},\qquad
\ell_{\rm cau}(\theta,m)=2\left\lfloor\frac m2\right\rfloor-1.
\tag{A2}\label{eq:pisot-linear-A2}
\end{equation*}
More precisely,
\begin{equation*}
\mathcal N_{\theta,m,\lambda_m-1}=\{E_m,-E_m\}\ne\varnothing,
\qquad
\mathcal N_{\theta,m,\lambda_m}=\varnothing.
\tag{A3}\label{eq:pisot-linear-A3}
\end{equation*}
Consequently
\[
\lim_{m\to\infty}\frac{\ell_{\rm cau}(\theta,m)}m=1.
\]
Moreover, in the bounded-zero graph $G_m(U_\theta,1)$ the maximum path
length from a first-coordinate-nonzero vertex is $\lambda_m-1$, hence at
least $m-3$.  Thus no uniform theorem for the fixed systems covered by
\textup{(A1)}, and no uniform analogue of
Theorem~\ref{thm:bounded-zero-linear-transient}, can replace the linear order
by $o(m)$.  This does not assert that the effective constants in those
results are numerically optimal or that coefficient one is universal across
systems.
\end{theorem}
NEW_THEOREM_4_1
\begin{theorem}[Fixed cubic sharpness and terminal classification]
\label{thm:fixed-cubic-sharpness}
For every $m\ge4$,
\begin{equation*}
\Phi_{\theta,m}\text{ is injective},\qquad
\ell_{\rm cau}(\theta,m)=2\left\lfloor\frac m2\right\rfloor-1.
\tag{A2}\label{eq:pisot-linear-A2}
\end{equation*}
More precisely,
\begin{equation*}
\mathcal N_{\theta,m,\lambda_m-1}=\{E_m,-E_m\}\ne\varnothing,
\qquad
\mathcal N_{\theta,m,\lambda_m}=\varnothing.
\tag{A3}\label{eq:pisot-linear-A3}
\end{equation*}
Consequently
\[
\lim_{m\to\infty}\frac{\ell_{\rm cau}(\theta,m)}m=1.
\]
Moreover, in the bounded-zero graph $G_m(U_\theta,1)$ the maximum path
length from a first-coordinate-nonzero vertex is $\lambda_m-1$, hence at
least $m-3$.  Thus no uniform theorem for the fixed systems covered by
\textup{(A1)}, and no uniform analogue of
Theorem~\ref{thm:bounded-zero-linear-transient}, can replace the linear order
by $o(m)$.
\end{theorem}
NEW_REMARKS_4
\begin{remark}[Simple-Parry input]
The identity $d_\theta(1)=1101\,0^\infty$ supplies the legal language and its
rank recurrence.  After that identification, the proof uses the recurrence
for $Q_j(\theta)$, a direct quotient bound, the contracting-conjugate
estimate, the suffix-sum identity, and the terminal integer equations.
\end{remark}
\begin{remark}[Order sharpness]
Sharpness here concerns growth order for the fixed recurrence $U_\theta$ and
the fixed coefficient alphabet $\{-1,0,1\}$.  The effective constants in
Theorem~\ref{thm:bounded-zero-linear-transient} and Corollary
\ref{cor:pisot-linear-causal-depth} are not claimed to be numerically
optimal across systems.
\end{remark}

1. VERBATIM: `sections/00_abstract.tex`

```tex
\begin{abstract}
We prove an effective linear bound for transients in one-position overlap
graphs of bounded representations of zero in a fixed Pisot linear numeration
system.  If $U=(u_j)$ is strictly increasing, $u_0=1$, and its recurrence
has a nonintegral Pisot characteristic root, then for every coefficient
bound $D$ there is an effective constant $C(U,D)$ such that, whenever no
directed cycle is reachable, every nonzero anchored path at aperture $m$ has
fewer than $C(U,D)m$ edges.  The proof compares adjacent zero
representations under the algebraic embeddings: contraction bounds the
nondominant contributions, and finite separation in the dominant embedding
forces the successive quotients to collapse.

For cyclic recodings obtained by reducing canonical language ranks modulo
$u_m$, the coordinate-difference graph is exactly this bounded-zero overlap
graph, so injectivity implies an aperture-linear future-only inverse depth.
For the fixed binary cubic recurrence with leading root of
$x^3-2x^2+x-1$, the recoding is injective for every $m\ge4$ and has exact
causal length $2\lfloor m/2\rfloor-1$; the same construction proves that the
linear transient bound is sharp in order.
\end{abstract}

\medskip
\noindent{\bf 2020 Mathematics Subject Classification.}
Primary: 37B10, 11B39; Secondary: 11A67, 37B40.

\medskip
\noindent{\bf Key words and phrases.}
Pisot numeration, bounded representation of zero, overlap graph, linear
transient, cyclic rank recoding, future-only inverse, collision quotient.
```

2. VERBATIM: Theorem A, Theorem B, and Theorem C

```tex
\begin{theoremA}
Let $U=(u_j)$ be strictly increasing with $u_0=1$, and suppose that its
integral recurrence has as characteristic polynomial the minimal polynomial
of a nonintegral Pisot number.  For every $D\ge1$ there is an effectively
computable $C(U,D)<\infty$ such that, at every aperture $m\ge2$, if no
directed cycle is reachable from a first-coordinate-nonzero vertex of the
bounded-zero overlap graph, then every path from such a vertex has fewer
than $C(U,D)m$ edges.
\end{theoremA}

\begin{theoremB}
Let $U$ be any strictly increasing Pisot numeration system with $u_0=1$,
canonical greedy digit set, and canonical normal words.  There is an
effectively computable $C_U<\infty$ such that, for every $m\ge2$, injectivity
of the cyclic language-rank recoding $\Phi_{U,m}$ implies
\[
\ell_{\rm cau}(U,m)\le C_Um.
\]
\end{theoremB}

\begin{theoremC}
Let $\theta$ be the leading root of $x^3-2x^2+x-1$ and let $U_\theta$ be the
binary recurrence beginning $1,2,4$.  For every $m\ge4$, the recoding
$\Phi_{\theta,m}$ is injective and
\[
\ell_{\rm cau}(\theta,m)=2\left\lfloor\frac m2\right\rfloor-1.
\]
The maximum path length from a first-coordinate-nonzero vertex of
$G_m(U_\theta,1)$ is $2\lfloor m/2\rfloor-2$, so the linear order in
Theorems~A and B is sharp.
\end{theoremC}
```

Theorem 2.2, old:

```tex
\begin{theorem}[Linear transient bound for bounded $U$-representations of zero]
\label{thm:bounded-zero-linear-transient}
Let $U=(u_n)_{n\ge0}$ be a strictly increasing sequence of positive integers
with $u_0=1$, satisfying the integral recurrence whose characteristic
polynomial is the minimal polynomial of a nonintegral Pisot number $\beta$.
Fix $D\ge1$.  For $m\ge2$, let $G_m(U,D)$ be the directed graph with vertex
set
\[
([-D,D]\cap\mathbb Z)^{m-1}.
\]
There is an edge
\[
(e_0,\ldots,e_{m-2})\longrightarrow
(e_1,\ldots,e_{m-2},e_{m-1})
\]
if and only if there is an integer $c$ such that
\begin{equation}
e_0u_0+e_1u_1+\cdots+e_{m-1}u_{m-1}+cu_m=0.
\label{eq:bounded-zero-overlap-edge-theorem}
\end{equation}
The completing coefficient is unique and necessarily satisfies
$|c|\le K_{U,D}$.
Then there is an effectively computable $C(U,D)<\infty$ such that, for every
$m\ge2$, if no directed cycle of $G_m(U,D)$ is reachable from a vertex whose
first coordinate is nonzero, every directed path beginning at such a vertex
has fewer than $C(U,D)m$ edges.

Equivalently, after shifting a chain so that its initial exposed coefficient
is nonzero, either it reaches a directed cycle, or it has fewer than
$C(U,D)m$ further overlaps.  The graphs are finite-state recognizers of these
overlap chains, and their edges are bounded $U$-representations of zero by
\eqref{eq:bounded-zero-overlap-edge-theorem}.
\end{theorem}
```

Theorem 2.2, new:

```tex
\begin{theorem}[Linear transient bound for bounded $U$-representations of zero]
\label{thm:bounded-zero-linear-transient}
Let $U=(u_n)_{n\ge0}$ be a strictly increasing sequence of positive integers
with $u_0=1$, satisfying the integral recurrence whose characteristic
polynomial is the minimal polynomial of a nonintegral Pisot number $\beta$.
Fix $D\ge1$, and let $G_m(U,D)$ be the graph defined above.  For every edge,
the completing coefficient is unique and necessarily satisfies
$|c|\le K_{U,D}$.
Then there is an effectively computable $C(U,D)<\infty$ such that, for every
$m\ge2$, if no directed cycle of $G_m(U,D)$ is reachable from a vertex whose
first coordinate is nonzero, every directed path beginning at such a vertex
has fewer than $C(U,D)m$ edges.

Equivalently, after shifting a chain so that its initial exposed coefficient
is nonzero, either it reaches a directed cycle, or it has fewer than
$C(U,D)m$ further overlaps.  The graphs are finite-state recognizers of these
overlap chains, and their edges are bounded $U$-representations of zero by
\eqref{eq:bounded-zero-overlap-edge}.
\end{theorem}
```

Corollary 2.3, old:

```tex
The theorem also gives the useful global chain dichotomy.  Consider all
bounded zero words of length $m+1$ that overlap one digit at a time.  Either
some chain with a nonzero coefficient has a finite prefix followed by a
periodic bounded zero representation, or every nonzero chain has fewer than
$(C(U,D)+1)m$ rows.
```

Corollary 2.3, new:

```tex
\begin{corollary}[Global chain dichotomy]
\label{cor:bounded-zero-global-dichotomy}
Under the hypotheses of Theorem~\ref{thm:bounded-zero-linear-transient}, fix
$m\ge2$ and consider all bounded zero words of length $m+1$ that overlap one
digit at a time.  Either some chain with a nonzero coefficient has a finite
prefix followed by a periodic bounded zero representation, or every nonzero
chain has fewer than $(C(U,D)+1)m$ rows.
\end{corollary}
```

Remark 2.4, old:

```tex
\begin{remark}[Logical boundary of the standard-object theorem]
\label{rem:bounded-zero-standard-object-boundary}
The statement and proof of Theorem~\ref{thm:bounded-zero-linear-transient}
use only a fixed Pisot recurrence, bounded integer coefficients, the
distinguished weight $u_m$, and one-position overlaps of bounded
$U$-representations of zero.  They use no canonical rank bijection,
legal-word alphabet, cyclic reduction map, positive/negative realization of
coefficients, image shift, or inverse decoder.  The distinguished modulus
$u_m$ and consecutive recurrence weights remain essential: replacing $u_m$
by an unrelated modulus destroys \eqref{eq:bounded-zero-adjacent-identity}.
The theorem therefore does not concern numerical $\beta$-normalization,
successor carry propagation, general synchronized transductions, general
right-closing maps, or a broad class of sliding block codes.
\end{remark}
```

Remark 2.4, new:

```tex
\begin{remark}[Arithmetic scope]
\label{rem:bounded-zero-standard-object-boundary}
Theorem~\ref{thm:bounded-zero-linear-transient} is independent of the cyclic
rank construction of Section~\ref{sec:cyclic-rank-application}.  Its
essential arithmetic features are the distinguished terminal weight $u_m$
and the use of consecutive recurrence weights; replacing $u_m$ by an
unrelated modulus removes the identity underlying Lemma
\ref{lem:bounded-zero-arbitrary-alphabet-collapse}.  These features are what
make the linear estimate available for the overlap graphs considered here.
\end{remark}
```

Theorem 4.1, old:

```tex
\begin{theorem}[Fixed cubic sharpness and terminal classification]
\label{thm:fixed-cubic-sharpness}
For every $m\ge4$,
\begin{equation*}
\Phi_{\theta,m}\text{ is injective},\qquad
\ell_{\rm cau}(\theta,m)=2\left\lfloor\frac m2\right\rfloor-1.
\tag{A2}\label{eq:pisot-linear-A2}
\end{equation*}
More precisely,
\begin{equation*}
\mathcal N_{\theta,m,\lambda_m-1}=\{E_m,-E_m\}\ne\varnothing,
\qquad
\mathcal N_{\theta,m,\lambda_m}=\varnothing.
\tag{A3}\label{eq:pisot-linear-A3}
\end{equation*}
Consequently
\[
\lim_{m\to\infty}\frac{\ell_{\rm cau}(\theta,m)}m=1.
\]
Moreover, in the bounded-zero graph $G_m(U_\theta,1)$ the maximum path
length from a first-coordinate-nonzero vertex is $\lambda_m-1$, hence at
least $m-3$.  Thus no uniform theorem for the fixed systems covered by
\textup{(A1)}, and no uniform analogue of
Theorem~\ref{thm:bounded-zero-linear-transient}, can replace the linear order
by $o(m)$.  This does not assert that the effective constants in those
results are numerically optimal or that coefficient one is universal across
systems.
\end{theorem}
```

Theorem 4.1, new:

```tex
\begin{theorem}[Fixed cubic sharpness and terminal classification]
\label{thm:fixed-cubic-sharpness}
For every $m\ge4$,
\begin{equation*}
\Phi_{\theta,m}\text{ is injective},\qquad
\ell_{\rm cau}(\theta,m)=2\left\lfloor\frac m2\right\rfloor-1.
\tag{A2}\label{eq:pisot-linear-A2}
\end{equation*}
More precisely,
\begin{equation*}
\mathcal N_{\theta,m,\lambda_m-1}=\{E_m,-E_m\}\ne\varnothing,
\qquad
\mathcal N_{\theta,m,\lambda_m}=\varnothing.
\tag{A3}\label{eq:pisot-linear-A3}
\end{equation*}
Consequently
\[
\lim_{m\to\infty}\frac{\ell_{\rm cau}(\theta,m)}m=1.
\]
Moreover, in the bounded-zero graph $G_m(U_\theta,1)$ the maximum path
length from a first-coordinate-nonzero vertex is $\lambda_m-1$, hence at
least $m-3$.  Thus no uniform theorem for the fixed systems covered by
\textup{(A1)}, and no uniform analogue of
Theorem~\ref{thm:bounded-zero-linear-transient}, can replace the linear order
by $o(m)$.
\end{theorem}
```

```tex
The base $\theta$ is also a canonical simple-Parry example:
$d_\theta(1)=1101\,0^\infty$.  That identification supplies the legal
language and its rank recurrence, but the proof of
Theorem~\ref{thm:fixed-cubic-sharpness} uses none of the general
simple-Parry quotient theory.  After the Parry word is checked, the argument
needs only the recurrence for $Q_j(\theta)$, a direct quotient bound, the
contracting-conjugate estimate, the suffix-sum identity, and the terminal
integer equations.
```

```tex
\begin{remark}[Order sharpness]
Sharpness here concerns growth order for the fixed recurrence $U_\theta$ and
the fixed coefficient alphabet $\{-1,0,1\}$.  The effective constants in
Theorem~\ref{thm:bounded-zero-linear-transient} and Corollary
\ref{cor:pisot-linear-causal-depth} are not claimed to be numerically

\end{remark}
```

(c) The terminal classification is present and complete in `sections/04_fixed_cubic_sharpness.tex:150–218`. It includes:

```tex
In $\{-1,0,1\}^4$ its complete solution set is
\begin{equation}
(0,0,0,0),\qquad \pm(1,1,1,-1).
\label{eq:theta-four-tail-table}
\end{equation}
```

```tex
With one more row, the
terminal equation is
\[
e_{m-2}+2e_{m-1}+4e_m=0,
\]
whose only solution in $\{-1,0,1\}^3$ is zero.  Backward propagation then
contradicts $e_0\ne0$.  Thus
$\mathcal N_{\theta,m,\lambda_m}$ is empty.
```

```tex
The terminal five-digit
equation has, up to sign, exactly the following solutions:
\begin{equation}
(0,0,0,0,0),\quad
(1,1,1,-1,0),\quad
(1,0,1,1,-1),\quad
(1,-1,-1,-1,1).
\label{eq:theta-five-tail-table}
\end{equation}
```

```tex
The zero tail and the last two nonzero tails instead force $e_2=0$, hence
$e_0=0$; only $\pm(1,1,1,-1,0)$ survive.  They give exactly the even
terminal word for aperture $m-1$ followed by zero, namely $\pm E_m$.
With one more row the terminal four-digit alternatives are
\eqref{eq:theta-four-tail-table}; now $m-5$ has even parity and its forced
zero propagates to $e_0$.  Hence the next obstruction set is empty.
```

```tex
For $m=4,5$, successive exact division in the two or three window
congruences gives
\[
\begin{array}{c|c|c|c}
m&Q_m&\mathcal N_{\theta,m,\lambda_m-1}
&\mathcal N_{\theta,m,\lambda_m}\\ \hline
4&12&\{\pm(1,-1,-1,-1,1)\}&\varnothing\\
5&21&\{\pm(1,-1,-1,-1,1,0)\}&\varnothing.
\end{array}
\]
```

1. VERBATIM: `sections/00_abstract.tex`

```tex
\begin{abstract}
We prove an effective linear bound for transients in one-position overlap
graphs of bounded representations of zero in a fixed Pisot linear numeration
system.  If $U=(u_j)$ is strictly increasing, $u_0=1$, and its recurrence
has a nonintegral Pisot characteristic root, then for every coefficient
bound $D$ there is an effective constant $C(U,D)$ such that, whenever no
directed cycle is reachable, every nonzero anchored path at aperture $m$ has
fewer than $C(U,D)m$ edges.  The proof compares adjacent zero
representations under the algebraic embeddings: contraction bounds the
nondominant contributions, and finite separation in the dominant embedding
forces the successive quotients to collapse.

For cyclic recodings obtained by reducing canonical language ranks modulo
$u_m$, the coordinate-difference graph is exactly this bounded-zero overlap
graph, so injectivity implies an aperture-linear future-only inverse depth.
For the fixed binary cubic recurrence with leading root of
$x^3-2x^2+x-1$, the recoding is injective for every $m\ge4$ and has exact
causal length $2\lfloor m/2\rfloor-1$; the same construction proves that the
linear transient bound is sharp in order.
\end{abstract}

\medskip
\noindent{\bf 2020 Mathematics Subject Classification.}
Primary: 37B10, 11B39; Secondary: 11A67, 37B40.

\medskip
\noindent{\bf Key words and phrases.}
Pisot numeration, bounded representation of zero, overlap graph, linear
transient, cyclic rank recoding, future-only inverse, collision quotient.
```

2. VERBATIM: Theorem A, Theorem B, and Theorem C

```tex
\begin{theoremA}
Let $U=(u_j)$ be strictly increasing with $u_0=1$, and suppose that its
integral recurrence has as characteristic polynomial the minimal polynomial
of a nonintegral Pisot number.  For every $D\ge1$ there is an effectively
computable $C(U,D)<\infty$ such that, at every aperture $m\ge2$, if no
directed cycle is reachable from a first-coordinate-nonzero vertex of the
bounded-zero overlap graph, then every path from such a vertex has fewer
than $C(U,D)m$ edges.
\end{theoremA}

\begin{theoremB}
Let $U$ be any strictly increasing Pisot numeration system with $u_0=1$,
canonical greedy digit set, and canonical normal words.  There is an
effectively computable $C_U<\infty$ such that, for every $m\ge2$, injectivity
of the cyclic language-rank recoding $\Phi_{U,m}$ implies
\[
\ell_{\rm cau}(U,m)\le C_Um.
\]
\end{theoremB}

\begin{theoremC}
Let $\theta$ be the leading root of $x^3-2x^2+x-1$ and let $U_\theta$ be the
binary recurrence beginning $1,2,4$.  For every $m\ge4$, the recoding
$\Phi_{\theta,m}$ is injective and
\[
\ell_{\rm cau}(\theta,m)=2\left\lfloor\frac m2\right\rfloor-1.
\]
The maximum path length from a first-coordinate-nonzero vertex of
$G_m(U_\theta,1)$ is $2\lfloor m/2\rfloor-2$, so the linear order in
Theorems~A and B is sharp.
\end{theoremC}
```

Reworded-equivalent versions:

Theorem 2.2, old:

```tex
\begin{theorem}[Linear transient bound for bounded $U$-representations of zero]
\label{thm:bounded-zero-linear-transient}
Let $U=(u_n)_{n\ge0}$ be a strictly increasing sequence of positive integers
with $u_0=1$, satisfying the integral recurrence whose characteristic
polynomial is the minimal polynomial of a nonintegral Pisot number $\beta$.
Fix $D\ge1$.  For $m\ge2$, let $G_m(U,D)$ be the directed graph with vertex
set
\[
([-D,D]\cap\mathbb Z)^{m-1}.
\]
There is an edge
\[
(e_0,\ldots,e_{m-2})\longrightarrow
(e_1,\ldots,e_{m-2},e_{m-1})
\]
if and only if there is an integer $c$ such that
\begin{equation}
e_0u_0+e_1u_1+\cdots+e_{m-1}u_{m-1}+cu_m=0.
\label{eq:bounded-zero-overlap-edge-theorem}
\end{equation}
The completing coefficient is unique and necessarily satisfies
$|c|\le K_{U,D}$.
Then there is an effectively computable $C(U,D)<\infty$ such that, for every
$m\ge2$, if no directed cycle of $G_m(U,D)$ is reachable from a vertex whose
first coordinate is nonzero, every directed path beginning at such a vertex
has fewer than $C(U,D)m$ edges.

Equivalently, after shifting a chain so that its initial exposed coefficient
is nonzero, either it reaches a directed cycle, or it has fewer than
$C(U,D)m$ further overlaps.  The graphs are finite-state recognizers of these
overlap chains, and their edges are bounded $U$-representations of zero by
\eqref{eq:bounded-zero-overlap-edge-theorem}.
\end{theorem}
```

Theorem 2.2, new:

```tex
\begin{theorem}[Linear transient bound for bounded $U$-representations of zero]
\label{thm:bounded-zero-linear-transient}
Let $U=(u_n)_{n\ge0}$ be a strictly increasing sequence of positive integers
with $u_0=1$, satisfying the integral recurrence whose characteristic
polynomial is the minimal polynomial of a nonintegral Pisot number $\beta$.
Fix $D\ge1$, and let $G_m(U,D)$ be the graph defined above.  For every edge,
the completing coefficient is unique and necessarily satisfies
$|c|\le K_{U,D}$.
Then there is an effectively computable $C(U,D)<\infty$ such that, for every
$m\ge2$, if no directed cycle of $G_m(U,D)$ is reachable from a vertex whose
first coordinate is nonzero, every directed path beginning at such a vertex
has fewer than $C(U,D)m$ edges.

Equivalently, after shifting a chain so that its initial exposed coefficient
is nonzero, either it reaches a directed cycle, or it has fewer than
$C(U,D)m$ further overlaps.  The graphs are finite-state recognizers of these
overlap chains, and their edges are bounded $U$-representations of zero by
\eqref{eq:bounded-zero-overlap-edge}.
\end{theorem}
```

Corollary 2.3, old:

```tex
The theorem also gives the useful global chain dichotomy.  Consider all
bounded zero words of length $m+1$ that overlap one digit at a time.  Either
some chain with a nonzero coefficient has a finite prefix followed by a
periodic bounded zero representation, or every nonzero chain has fewer than
$(C(U,D)+1)m$ rows.
```

Corollary 2.3, new:

```tex
\begin{corollary}[Global chain dichotomy]
\label{cor:bounded-zero-global-dichotomy}
Under the hypotheses of Theorem~\ref{thm:bounded-zero-linear-transient}, fix
$m\ge2$ and consider all bounded zero words of length $m+1$ that overlap one
digit at a time.  Either some chain with a nonzero coefficient has a finite
prefix followed by a periodic bounded zero representation, or every nonzero
chain has fewer than $(C(U,D)+1)m$ rows.
\end{corollary}
```

Remark 2.4, old:

```tex
\begin{remark}[Logical boundary of the standard-object theorem]
\label{rem:bounded-zero-standard-object-boundary}
The statement and proof of Theorem~\ref{thm:bounded-zero-linear-transient}
use only a fixed Pisot recurrence, bounded integer coefficients, the
distinguished weight $u_m$, and one-position overlaps of bounded
$U$-representations of zero.  They use no canonical rank bijection,
legal-word alphabet, cyclic reduction map, positive/negative realization of
coefficients, image shift, or inverse decoder.  The distinguished modulus
$u_m$ and consecutive recurrence weights remain essential: replacing $u_m$
by an unrelated modulus destroys \eqref{eq:bounded-zero-adjacent-identity}.
The theorem therefore does not concern numerical $\beta$-normalization,
successor carry propagation, general synchronized transductions, general
right-closing maps, or a broad class of sliding block codes.
\end{remark}
```

Remark 2.4, new:

```tex
\begin{remark}[Arithmetic scope]
\label{rem:bounded-zero-standard-object-boundary}
Theorem~\ref{thm:bounded-zero-linear-transient} is independent of the cyclic
rank construction of Section~\ref{sec:cyclic-rank-application}.  Its
essential arithmetic features are the distinguished terminal weight $u_m$
and the use of consecutive recurrence weights; replacing $u_m$ by an
unrelated modulus removes the identity underlying Lemma
\ref{lem:bounded-zero-arbitrary-alphabet-collapse}.  These features are what
make the linear estimate available for the overlap graphs considered here.
\end{remark}
```

Theorem 4.1, old:

```tex
\begin{theorem}[Fixed cubic sharpness and terminal classification]
\label{thm:fixed-cubic-sharpness}
For every $m\ge4$,
\begin{equation*}
\Phi_{\theta,m}\text{ is injective},\qquad
\ell_{\rm cau}(\theta,m)=2\left\lfloor\frac m2\right\rfloor-1.
\tag{A2}\label{eq:pisot-linear-A2}
\end{equation*}
More precisely,
\begin{equation*}
\mathcal N_{\theta,m,\lambda_m-1}=\{E_m,-E_m\}\ne\varnothing,
\qquad
\mathcal N_{\theta,m,\lambda_m}=\varnothing.
\tag{A3}\label{eq:pisot-linear-A3}
\end{equation*}
Consequently
\[
\lim_{m\to\infty}\frac{\ell_{\rm cau}(\theta,m)}m=1.
\]
Moreover, in the bounded-zero graph $G_m(U_\theta,1)$ the maximum path
length from a first-coordinate-nonzero vertex is $\lambda_m-1$, hence at
least $m-3$.  Thus no uniform theorem for the fixed systems covered by
\textup{(A1)}, and no uniform analogue of
Theorem~\ref{thm:bounded-zero-linear-transient}, can replace the linear order
by $o(m)$.  This does not assert that the effective constants in those
results are numerically optimal or that coefficient one is universal across
systems.
\end{theorem}
```

Theorem 4.1, new:

```tex
\begin{theorem}[Fixed cubic sharpness and terminal classification]
\label{thm:fixed-cubic-sharpness}
For every $m\ge4$,
\begin{equation*}
\Phi_{\theta,m}\text{ is injective},\qquad
\ell_{\rm cau}(\theta,m)=2\left\lfloor\frac m2\right\rfloor-1.
\tag{A2}\label{eq:pisot-linear-A2}
\end{equation*}
More precisely,
\begin{equation*}
\mathcal N_{\theta,m,\lambda_m-1}=\{E_m,-E_m\}\ne\varnothing,
\qquad
\mathcal N_{\theta,m,\lambda_m}=\varnothing.
\tag{A3}\label{eq:pisot-linear-A3}
\end{equation*}
Consequently
\[
\lim_{m\to\infty}\frac{\ell_{\rm cau}(\theta,m)}m=1.
\]
Moreover, in the bounded-zero graph $G_m(U_\theta,1)$ the maximum path
length from a first-coordinate-nonzero vertex is $\lambda_m-1$, hence at
least $m-3$.  Thus no uniform theorem for the fixed systems covered by
\textup{(A1)}, and no uniform analogue of
Theorem~\ref{thm:bounded-zero-linear-transient}, can replace the linear order
by $o(m)$.
\end{theorem}
```

Remark 4.2, old source formulation:

```tex
The base $\theta$ is also a canonical simple-Parry example:
$d_\theta(1)=1101\,0^\infty$.  That identification supplies the legal
language and its rank recurrence, but the proof of
Theorem~\ref{thm:fixed-cubic-sharpness} uses none of the general
simple-Parry quotient theory.  After the Parry word is checked, the argument
needs only the recurrence for $Q_j(\theta)$, a direct quotient bound, the
contracting-conjugate estimate, the suffix-sum identity, and the terminal
integer equations.
```

Remark 4.2, new:

```tex
\begin{remark}[Simple-Parry input]
The identity $d_\theta(1)=1101\,0^\infty$ supplies the legal language and its
rank recurrence.  After that identification, the proof uses the recurrence
for $Q_j(\theta)$, a direct quotient bound, the contracting-conjugate
estimate, the suffix-sum identity, and the terminal integer equations.
\end{remark}
```

```tex
This does not assert that the effective constants in those

```

Remark 4.3, new:

```tex
\begin{remark}[Order sharpness]
Sharpness here concerns growth order for the fixed recurrence $U_\theta$ and
the fixed coefficient alphabet $\{-1,0,1\}$.  The effective constants in
Theorem~\ref{thm:bounded-zero-linear-transient} and Corollary
\ref{cor:pisot-linear-causal-depth} are not claimed to be numerically
optimal across systems.
\end{remark}
```

(c) The terminal classification is present and complete in `sections/04_fixed_cubic_sharpness.tex:150–218`. It includes:

```tex
In $\{-1,0,1\}^4$ its complete solution set is
\begin{equation}
(0,0,0,0),\qquad \pm(1,1,1,-1).
\label{eq:theta-four-tail-table}
\end{equation}
```

```tex
With one more row, the
terminal equation is
\[
e_{m-2}+2e_{m-1}+4e_m=0,
\]
whose only solution in $\{-1,0,1\}^3$ is zero.  Backward propagation then
contradicts $e_0\ne0$.  Thus
$\mathcal N_{\theta,m,\lambda_m}$ is empty.
```

```tex
The terminal five-digit
equation has, up to sign, exactly the following solutions:
\begin{equation}
(0,0,0,0,0),\quad
(1,1,1,-1,0),\quad
(1,0,1,1,-1),\quad
(1,-1,-1,-1,1).
\label{eq:theta-five-tail-table}
\end{equation}
```

```tex
The zero tail and the last two nonzero tails instead force $e_2=0$, hence
$e_0=0$; only $\pm(1,1,1,-1,0)$ survive.  They give exactly the even
terminal word for aperture $m-1$ followed by zero, namely $\pm E_m$.
With one more row the terminal four-digit alternatives are
\eqref{eq:theta-four-tail-table}; now $m-5$ has even parity and its forced
zero propagates to $e_0$.  Hence the next obstruction set is empty.
```

```tex
For $m=4,5$, successive exact division in the two or three window
congruences gives
\[
\begin{array}{c|c|c|c}
m&Q_m&\mathcal N_{\theta,m,\lambda_m-1}
&\mathcal N_{\theta,m,\lambda_m}\\ \hline
4&12&\{\pm(1,-1,-1,-1,1)\}&\varnothing\\
5&21&\{\pm(1,-1,-1,-1,1,0)\}&\varnothing.
\end{array}
\]
```
